Company Overview: Eternal at a glance  |  Statutory Reports  |  Financial Statements
Eternal at a glance
Eternal is more than just a name - it is a commitment to building something that deserves to last. An
institution where culture outlasts strategy, where systems outlast individuals, where the work of one
generation creates the foundation for the next. Today, that foundation spans four key businesses
transforming how Indians dine, shop and go-out.

Company Overview: Our key businesses  |  Statutory Reports  |  Financial Statements
A. Food delivery
Zomato operates a B2C technology platform
that provides customers with a seamless, on-
demand solution to discover local restaurants,
order food, and have it delivered reliably and
quickly.
Every order on Zomato requires real-time
coordination. A meal has to be prepared by a
third-party restaurant, picked up by an
independent delivery partner navigating
unpredictable traffic, and delivered while the
food is still hot  executed at a scale of millions
of orders per day with no room for error. Over
time, we have invested in building systems that make this possible, reliably and at scale across the length
and breadth of the country. As of Mar-26, our food delivery operations were present across 900+ cities pan-
India.

We also run a membership program called Zomato Gold which provides members with multiple benefits
including free delivery on orders and exclusive offers, subject to terms. As of Mar-26, we had 24.2 million
active Gold members on our platform.

Company Overview: Our key businesses  |  Statutory Reports  |  Financial Statements
B. Quick commerce
Blinkit is a quick commerce B2C platform
providing on-demand delivery of products
across multiple categories. Customers can
place orders on the Blinkit app and have them
delivered to their doorstep. Orders are fulfilled
through a network of stores located typically
within a 3 km radius from the customer and are
delivered by a last mile delivery fleet of
independent delivery partners. Blinkit offers a
wide assortment including daily essentials,
electronics, beauty & personal care, home
décor, toys & games, sports accessories,
general merchandise and more.
FY26 key metrics

In FY26, we transitioned the quick commerce business from a marketplace model to inventory ownership.
Owning inventory gives us greater control over assortment depth, margins and lets us expand assortment
faster, making our business more durable over time. By Mar-26, ~90% of our quick commerce NOV was on our
own inventory, which we expect to be the steady state going forward.
Blinkit continued to expand its geographical footprint during the fiscal and added 942 net new stores to its
network. We also expanded our backend warehousing infrastructure to support this growth. As of Mar-26,
Blinkit managed a total of 8.5 million sqft. of warehousing space.

C. Going-out
Going-out is our third B2C business which
addresses the ˘going-outˇ needs of our customers
by enabling discovery and transactions across
multiple going-out experiences including dining-
out, movies, sporting events, live events, concerts,
outdoor activities and shopping through the
District app.
FY26 was the first full year of District operating as a
unified going-out platform. No other platform in
India brings together this breadth of going-out use
cases on a single app. As we add more use cases,
engagement frequency and customer retention
improve, with each category reinforcing the others.

Company Overview: Our key businesses  |  Statutory Reports  |  Financial Statements
During FY26, we launched four new initiatives:
District Stores: digitizing offline retail by enabling discovery of stores with access to deals and offers;
onboarded ~5,200 outlets across 8 cities by Mar-26
District Play: entered recreational sports by enabling discovery and booking of third-party sports
facilities on a pay-and-play basis
District Pass: launched our loyalty membership program offering benefits across movies and dining-out,
to drive customer retention and incremental demand across all going-out use cases on District
UAE expansion: launched District app in UAE bringing dining-out and live events onto a single app

Company Overview: Our key businesses  |  Statutory Reports  |  Financial Statements
D. B2B supplies
Hyperpure is our B2B supplies business
offering quality food ingredients and other
products to restaurants. Hyperpure operates a
1P model (i.e., owns inventory) where it sources
directly from farmer producer organizations,
traders and brands, and supplies to its
customers through its end-to-end supply chain.
In addition, Hyperpure has also been scaling
two new verticals  a) value added food supplies
through its own processing facility for value
added products such as sauces, spreads, pre-
cut and semi-finished perishables, and b) 4PL
warehousing supply chain services offering
end-to-end logistics and supply chain management for restaurants including procurement, warehousing and
final supply, currently serving multiple large branded chains with a pan-India presence. Customers choose
Hyperpure as it acts as a single destination offering consistently high quality products with competitive pricing
across categories and reliable & timely delivery.
FY26 key metrics
# of warehouses (Mar-26)
INR 5,366 crore
130,000+
11
YoY change
YoY growth
Across 8 cities
13%
25%
Up until FY26, Hyperpure also had a non-restaurant business comprising B2B sales of mainly fresh food and
staples to the sellers on Blinkit marketplace. This business scaled down completely during the fiscal year
driven by shift to inventory ownership in quick commerce and hence, Hyperpureˇs total Revenue saw a YoY
decline. However, the core restaurant business continued to scale and grew 38% YoY in FY26.
Hyperpure also fulfils a core capability for each of our B2C businesses -
Food delivery and going-out (dining): For restaurants, Hyperpure removes the daily stress of sourcing
and inventory management. Chefs and operators get fresh, reliable ingredients every time without
needing large storage space or a dedicated procurement team. This enables restaurants to run leaner
operations, scale faster, and focus more on delivering a great experience to their customers. Over time,
this kind of reliability builds trust, which then extends to our relationship with these restaurants in both
these businesses
Quick commerce: Blinkit relies on Hyperpureˇs sourcing engine for fresh and perishable products. Its technology-driven, backward-integrated supply chain and warehousing infrastructure enables Blinkit to offer consistent and quality SKUs while maintaining healthy sourcing margins and minimising wastage

Company Overview: Our key businesses  |  Statutory Reports  |  Financial Statements
E. Others
Others segment primarily includes new initiatives that we may launch from time to time. As of Mar-26, Others
segment included Bistro, Nugget, Greening India, amongst others.
Bistro: (
Bistro is a quick food delivery service where it
controls the entire supply chain from kitchen
infrastructure to food preparation to last mile
delivery, which allows it to bring down food costs
materially. The intent is to focus on two demand
pockets that are currently unaddressed by the
existing restaurant and food delivery ecosystem - a)
customers looking for high quality but low cost meals
and, b) customers looking for instant snacky food.
Nugget:
Nugget is our AI platform for customer operations,
with agents capable of not only handling conversations
but also driving processes to completion across
channels. Nugget powers 15+ million interactions a
month across Zomato, Blinkit, District and Hyperpure,
with 85%+ resolved end-to-end without human
intervention. In FY26, Nugget was piloted with select
external customers.
In addition to the above, Others segment also includes Zomato Payments Private Limited (ZPPL) and Zomato
Local Services Private Limited (ZLSPL).
11

FY26 was the first year when our NOV (B2C business) crossed $10 billion - a milestone that took us 18
years to reach. We expect annual NOV to double to $20 billion in the next two years.

B2B business Hyperpureˇs Revenue declined 13% YoY to INR 5,366 crore as the non-restaurant
business scaled down following the shift to inventory ownership in quick commerce.
Consolidated Adjusted Revenue grew 159% YoY to INR 55,903 crore in FY26. The sharp increase was
primarily driven by the shift to inventory ownership in quick commerce where Revenue now includes
the full monetary value of goods sold (and not just the marketplace commission). On a like-for-like
basis (excluding the impact of inventory ownership shift), Adjusted Revenue grew 61% YoY.

˘Ind AS 116 leasesˇ. EBITDA grew faster than Adjusted EBITDA as ESOP expense remained flattish with only 3% YoY increase, while the rent pertaining to ˘Ind AS 116 leasesˇ gre grew rapidly in line

increase in consolidated Adjusted EBITDA. Decrease in PAT was mainly due to increase in depreciation
& amortisation expense in the quick commerce business.

Food delivery Orders

Food delivery NOV grew 16% YoY to INR 37,993 crore in FY26, driven by 15% YoY growth in Orders
while NAOV remained broadly flat (0.7% YoY growth). Order volume growth was largely driven by
growth in average monthly transacting customers (MTC), which grew 18% YoY to 24.3 million in
FY26.
NOV growth recovered progressively through the year after bottoming out in Q1FY26, with Q4FY26
NOV growth accelerating to 19% YoY. The recovery was driven by an improvement in the overall
demand environment and our continued focus on expanding the addressable market into more
price-sensitive segments. During FY26, we lowered the minimum order value for free delivery
under the Gold program from INR 199 to INR 99 and stepped up targeted customer activation.
We expect YoY NOV growth to continue trending towards our long-term expectation of 20%+ over
time, driven by gradual market share gains and the compounding impact of continued investments
in affordability and selection.

B. Quick commerce
Quick commerce became our largest B2C business in FY26, with
NOV growing 117% YoY

NAOV (INR)
Quick commerce NOV grew 117% YoY to INR 48,567 crore in FY26, surpassing food delivery NOV
scale for the full year. NOV growth was driven by 116% YoY growth in Orders while NAOV remained
broadly flat (0.3% YoY growth). Order volume growth was driven by 117% YoY increase in average
monthly transacting customers, on the back of continued investments in geographic expansion
and customer acquisition.
Blinkit has scaled at a NOV CAGR of 104% since FY23 (year of Blinkit acquisition). The long-term
opportunity remains significant, and we expect NOV to grow at 60%+ CAGR over the next three
years. This growth will be driven by assortment expansion, geographic coverage and demand
densification while improving the quality and reliability of our service.

Blinkit added 942 net new stores in FY26, taking the total count to 2,243 stores across 250+ cities as
of Mar-26. Store additions were driven by expansion into new localities and increasing network
density in existing areas where demand was exceeding capacity.
To support this rapid expansion, we added ~3.3 million sqft. of backend warehousing space during
FY26, taking the total warehousing footprint to 8.5 million sqft. as of Mar-26. We remain on track to
achieve our target of ~3,000 stores by Mar-27.
Despite the accelerated pace of store additions, average NOV per day, per store remained broadly
stable at INR 756k in FY26.

includes the full monetary value of goods sold (and not just the marketplace commission).

state level. Accordingly, FY26 quick commerce Revenue is not comparable with previous years.

Going-out NOV grew 42% YoY to INR 9,399 crore in FY26 from INR 6,618 crore in FY25, driven by
continued growth across all key categories including dining-out, movies, sports and live events.
Revenue grew 32% YoY to INR 973 crore in FY26.
During FY26, we added new categories such as shopping (local retail), launched District in the UAE
by bringing our existing dining-out business and live event ticketing on a single app and introduced
District Pass as a common loyalty program across multiple going-out use cases. Together, these
initiatives strengthened the District ecosystem and supported NOV growth during the fiscal.

Company Overview: ESG update  |  Statutory Reports  |  Financial Statements
Our updated materiality matrix
We conducted our first comprehensive materiality assessment in FY22 to identify our key sustainability
themes. The eight themes identified were reflective of the company we were at the time. Food delivery was
our single large business, complemented by a dining-out offering and a much smaller Hyperpure business.
Since then, the organization has grown multi-fold. Quick commerce, which did not exist as a business for us
in FY22, is now our largest business by NOV. Dining-out has grown into a full-fledged going-out offering
spanning dining, events, movies, shopping and other use cases. Hyperpure has scaled meaningfully and now
supports each of our B2C businesses. With this evolution, our stakeholder universe has also broadened and
so have the ESG themes that are relevant to us.
To align our ESG priorities with the business we are today, we refreshed our materiality assessment in FY26
through a double materiality assessment covering all four key businesses. A double materiality lens
evaluates each topic on two dimensions:
Impact our businesses have on people and the environment
Financial implications of sustainability-related issues on our businesses
Nine material topics were identified through this assessment.€

The goals we set under our earlier framework in Jan-23 were defined for the food delivery business, which
was our only large business at the time. We made good progress on many of those goals. For example, we
have maintained 100% plastic neutral food deliveries by recycling as much plastic as restaurants use for
packing their food delivery orders, delivered 100 million 'low plastic packaging' orders from 10k+ restaurants
and achieved our target of serving 300,000+ MSME restaurants and creating economic opportunities for
over 1 million delivery partners. We also achieved our goal of serving 300 million meals through Feeding India
in Jun-26, well before our initial timeline of 2030.
We are now crafting new goals relevant to our expanded portfolio of businesses and aligned with the material
topics identified in our latest assessment. As part of this exercise, we also intend to revise our goals around
100% EV-based deliveries and Net Zero (covered under Pillar 1 below).

Circular economy

initiative since launch
Re-evaluation of EV and Net Zero€go al s
Our focused efforts on driving EV-based last-mile deliveries over the last few years has led to 2W EV fleet
penetration reaching an estimated 2x+ of the on-road penetration of 2W EVs in India. Our learnings through
this journey point to three constraints holding back mass EV adoption among delivery partners. With
deliveries contributing significantly to our scope 3 emissions, EV adoption is central to our Net Zero goal:
High upfront costs vs. low full-time participation: A majority of delivery partners work part-time to
supplement existing incomes and prefer to use vehicles they already own or have access to. Investing in
a new EV is viable only for a small share of delivery partners with long-term delivery plans
High need for credit but limited and expensive financing: Most of the delivery partners interested in
buying an EV require financing, but credit availability is limited. Those who can access financing often
face unfavorable loan terms (higher interest rates, lower loan-to-value ratios) compared to ICE vehicles
Charging infrastructure gaps: Inadequate charging and swapping infrastructure outside major cities,
e.g., hometowns of delivery partners, creates range anxiety and undermines partners’ confidence in the
long-term viability of owning an EV
These are ecosystem-level constraints. Solving them requires coordinated effort across multiple
aggregators like us, EV manufacturers, infrastructure and financing players from both public and private
sectors to facilitate a just, orderly and equitable transition away from petrol-based vehicles. We continue to
contribute through awareness-building, enabling access to rental EVs and advocacy via government
consultations and industry coalitions.
We are working with experts and partners to craft new sustainable transportation goals that better reflect
our growth, diversification and on-ground realities. This exercise will lead to a revision of both of our 100%
EV-based deliveries and Net Zero goals. We will share the outcome once it concludes.

Pillar 2
Inclusive growth
Reaching customers at scale has, traditionally,
required investments most small businesses cannot
afford. Our platforms lower these barriers, giving
small businesses and upcoming entrepreneurs
access to millions of customers and enabling them to
compete with larger players on a level playing field. In
this process, we also help generate income
opportunities for lakhs of riders and merchant
partners.
We intend to deepen this over time by (i) helping
more small businesses find growth on our platforms,
(ii) expanding earning opportunities for delivery
partners and (iii) investing in the development of our
own people, whose capabilities ultimately determine
how well we serve everyone else. Our work under this
pillar spans four themes. The table below
summarizes our key initiatives and progress under
each theme in FY26.

Pillar 3
Responsible business leadership
For us, business leadership carries a dual mandate: (i)
innovating ahead of evolving stakeholder expectations
and, (ii) protecting the health, safety and dignity of
everyone our businesses touch, including beyond our
value chain through initiatives like Feeding India and
Blinkit Ambulance service. At the same time, we also
work to preserve the quality of the food across our
operations while safeguarding the trust our brands
have earned.
Our work under this pillar spans five themes. The table
below summarizes our key initiatives and progress
under each theme in FY26.

Company Overview: ESG update  |  Statutory Reports  |  Financial Statements
Beyond our value chain
Even as a young company, we have tried to give back to
society through initiatives that sit outside our value chain.
Feeding India (FI), is an example of that.
Feeding India currently serves over 1.4 lakh children daily
across 2,300+ schools and Anganwadis and has served ~300
million meals since inception. In FY26, FI leveraged technology
to build higher trust with donors. A mobile application verifies
each child through facial recognition attendance and validates
each meal as it is received, ensuring every donated rupee
translates into a verified meal. Donors can search 'Feeding
India' on Zomato and Blinkit apps to see the number of meals
their contributions have funded.
Daily
feeding
program by
In FY26, FI also extended its effort beyond providing meals to improving the spaces where those meals are
served. Anganwadis serve as the first point of contact between India's underprivileged children and the
public care system, yet many operate out of rooms that were never built for the purpose. In FY26, FI rebuilt
45 of them across Varanasi, Nandurbar, Gurugram and Kushinagar into dedicated care centres. These
centres are safe, functional spaces where children can be served meals, taught and looked after the way the
system intends. Separately, when floods struck Punjab in September 2025, FI worked with local NGO
partners to distribute 5,250 relief kits to the affected families.
We also scaled the Blinkit Ambulance service during the fiscal year. In FY26, this service provided
emergency medical assistance in 10,000+ cases.

other three pillars rest. The Report on Corporate

FY26 key highlights:
Launched online Code of Conduct training through
our learning platform in May 2025, supported by an
annual employee sign-off
Reported zero data breaches in FY26
Continued to hold ISO 27001:2022 certification for the management of information security
Scaled AI adoption across our businesses: We are amongst the largest deployers of AI in India. We use it
to expand our addressable market and improve efficiency across our operations
Adopted responsible AI practices: AI outputs are clearly labeled, models are monitored for accuracy and
bias, data is kept in secure, access-controlled environments and all employees are trained on responsible
use of AI

Company Overview  |  Statutory Reports: MD&A  |  Financial Statements
A.
Income
Consolidated revenue from operations increased 169% YoY to INR 54,364 crore in FY26. The sharp increase
was primarily driven by the shift to inventory ownership in the quick commerce business where revenue now
includes the full monetary value of goods sold (and not just the marketplace commission).
a) Food delivery revenue from operations grew 26% YoY to INR 10,159 crore in FY26, primarily driven by
higher order volumes and increase in revenue per order. Revenue per order increased due to higher
commission income, increase in platform fee and better ad monetization. Platform fee grew faster than food
delivery revenue due to increase in average platform fee per order in FY26 compared to FY25.
(Note: Food delivery revenue as per the financial statements is different from the food delivery Adjusted
Revenue that we have presented on prior pages of this report as that also includes the customer delivery
charges that we collect on behalf of delivery partners from customers (net of any discounts, including free
delivery discounts on account of Zomato Gold program) and platform fee and other charges paid by the
customers on food delivery orders that are not already included in revenue from operations. Adjusted Revenue
is a metric tracked by the management to analyse the business performance. Refer page 49 for the
reconciliation between consolidated Adjusted Revenue and revenue from operations.)
b) Quick commerce revenue grew 626% YoY to INR 37,779 crore in FY26 primarily driven by shift to inventory
ownership in quick commerce from Q1FY26 onwards, where revenue now also includes the full monetary
value of goods sold (and not just the marketplace commission). On a like-for-like basis1, quick commerce
revenue grew 144% YoY.
c) Going-out revenue grew 32% YoY to INR 973 crore in FY26 driven by growth across all categories including
dining-out, movies and events. FY26 was also the first full financial year post acquisition of entertainment
ticketing business in FY25.
d) Hyperpure revenue declined 13% YoY to INR 5,366 crore in FY26, driven by scale down of the non-
restaurant business following the shift to inventory ownership in quick commerce. The core restaurant
supplies business grew 38% YoY in FY26, driven primarily by an increase in the number of restaurant outlets
served.
Other income increased by INR 319 crore to INR 1,396 crore in FY26, primarily driven by higher treasury
income. While the closing cash balance declined YoY in FY26 on account of capex and NWC investments in
the quick commerce business, the average investable cash balance remained higher than FY25 as the QIP
proceeds received in November 2024 were available for the full year (versus only part of FY25). Treasury
yields remained broadly stable YoY.
Note:
Like-for-like (
˝LFL˛) ) quick commerce revenue is calculated as quick commerce revenue from operations (-) cost of goods sold in case of own
inventory sales.
46


Company Overview  |  Statutory Reports: MD&A  |  Financial Statements
B.
Expense
Cost of goods sold (COGS) increased by 442% YoY to INR 30,167 crore in FY26 primarily due to shift to
inventory ownership in our quick commerce business.
Employee benefits expense increased 38% YoY to INR 3,536 crore in FY26. This primarily includes salaries,
wages, bonuses and share-based compensation paid to our on-roll employees across all our businesses.
Increase in expense was largely driven by higher salaries & wages, which grew YoY due to (i) impact of annual
increments, (ii) 33% YoY increase in headcount to 22,470 in FY26 and (iii) full-year impact of consolidation of
employees onboarded as part of the acquisition of the entertainment ticketing business in FY25. Increase in
headcount was largely due to team expansion in the quick commerce and going-out business segments.
Share-based payment expense grew 3% YoY to INR 819 crore in FY26.
Finance costs increased 155% YoY to INR 392 crore in FY26. This primarily includes interest on lease
liabilities recorded under Ind AS 116 which increased YoY due to the expansion in store and warehouse
network in the quick commerce business.
Depreciation and amortisation expenses grew 85% YoY to INR 1,597 crore in FY26. This includes (i)
depreciation on fixed assets, (ii) depreciation as recorded under Ind AS 116 and (iii) amortisation of intangible
assets. Increase in expense was primarily driven by higher depreciation on fixed assets and under Ind AS 116
due to the continued expansion of store and warehouse network in the quick commerce business.
Amortisation expense increased 7% YoY due to the full year impact of amortisation of intangible assets
created due to the acquisition of the entertainment ticketing business in FY25.

47


Company Overview  |  Statutory Reports: MD&A  |  Financial Statements
Delivery and related charges increased 58% YoY in FY26 to INR 9,065 crore. This includes (i) payouts1 to
delivery partners for last mile deliveries, (ii) delivery partner support cost and (iii) cost of consumables issued
to delivery partners at the time of onboarding. Increase in expense was largely due to increase in delivery
partner payouts in the food delivery and quick commerce businesses due to the growth in order volumes.
Delivery partner support cost and cost of consumables also increased YoY due to increase in overall order
volumes and delivery partners on the platform.
Advertisement and sales promotion expenses increased 70% YoY to INR 3,350 crore in FY26. This includes
(i) platform funded subsidies (to the extent not netted off from revenue), (ii) marketing & branding costs, (iii)
customer appeasement costs and, (iv) refunds across our business operations. Increase in expense was
primarily driven by higher marketing & branding costs and promotional spends. Marketing and branding costs
increased primarily due to growth investments in the quick commerce and going-out business, including
higher digital marketing spends to drive customer growth. Promotional spends increased primarily due to
investments in targeted activation of price sensitive customer cohorts in the food delivery business and
driving customer acquisition and engagement in the going-out business, including the launch of District
Pass.
IT support services, server and communication costs increased 69% YoY to INR 1,091 crore in FY26. This
primarily includes server hire charges, software subscription cost and communication costs incurred for our
consolidated operations. Increase in expense was primarily driven by higher cloud computing costs due to
increase in order/ transaction volumes across our B2C businesses.
Outsourced support cost increased 98% YoY to INR 1,131 crore in FY26. This primarily includes costs related
to (i) store and warehouse related off-roll manpower costs in our quick commerce operations and (ii) support
costs across our consolidated operations. Increase in expense was largely driven by increase in off-roll
manpower deployed across stores in the quick commerce business. Support cost decreased YoY as we
scaled Nugget.
Payment gateway charges increased 25% YoY to INR 285 crore in FY26 primarily driven by increase in NOV
transacted across our B2C platforms.
Others expense increased 94% YoY to INR 4,531 crore in FY26. This primarily includes quick commerce
warehouse management & associated logistics cost, rental expenses, legal & professional fee, general &
admin expenses, insurance cost, amongst others. Increase in expense was largely driven by increase in costs
associated with our quick commerce business including rentals & utilities, warehouse management
expenses, freight and logistics expense (excluding last mile cost) and others. Part of the increase was also
due to growth in live event costs (e.g., artist and venue booking) in case of events co-produced, managed and
marketed by the Company. Other expenses such as legal & professional fee, G&A expenses also grew YoY.
Note:
Comprises of (i) availability fees paid by Zomato to the delivery partner over and above the customer delivery charge collected on behalf of delivery
partners in the food delivery business and (ii) delivery partner payouts in the quick commerce business.

2. State of the Company’s affairs
Company overview
The Company is one of the first home-grown new-age
tech companies listed in India and operates through
four key business segments:

Food delivery: A technology platform that
provides customers with a seamless, on-demand
solution to search and discover restaurants,
order food, and have it delivered reliably
and quickly.

Quick commerce: Platform offering doorstep
delivery of products across categories
(fresh, staples, electronics, beauty, general
merchandise, festive needs ++).

Going-out: Going-out segment addresses the
‘going-out’ needs of our customers and enables
discovery and transactions for large going-out
experiences including dining-out, movies, sports
& other live events, shopping etc.

B2B supplies: B2B business supplying quality food
ingredients and other products to restaurants.

Financial Statements
Statutory Reports: Board Report
Company Overview


Financial results
Consolidated revenue from operations grew 169%
YoY to INR 54,364 crore in FY26 from INR 20,243 crore
in FY25 primarily driven by shift to inventory model
in quick commerce where revenue now also includes
the full monetary value of goods sold (and not just the
marketplace commission):

Food delivery revenue grew 26% YoY to INR 10,159
crore in FY26, primarily driven by higher order
volumes and increase in revenue per order.

Quick commerce revenue grew 626% YoY to INR
37,779 crore in FY26 primarily driven by shift to
inventory model where revenue now also includes
the full monetary value of goods sold (and not
just the marketplace commission).

Going-out revenue grew 32% YoY to INR 973 crore
in FY26 driven by growth across all categories
including dining-out, movies and events.
FY26 was also the first full financial year post
acquisition of entertainment ticketing business
in FY25.

B2B supplies revenue declined 13% YoY to INR
5,366 crore in FY26, driven by scale down of
the non-restaurant business following the shift
to inventory model in quick commerce. Core
restaurant supplies business continued to grow
YoY.
Consolidated Adjusted EBITDA improved to INR
1,189 crore in FY26 from INR 1,079 crore in FY25.
Improvement in Adjusted EBITDA profitability was
primarily driven by (a) improvement in food delivery
Adjusted EBITDA margin and (b) reduction in losses
in our quick commerce business and B2B supplies
business.
Consolidated EBITDA for the full fiscal year was
positive INR 1,208 crore. Consolidated PAT declined
to INR 366 crore in FY26 compared to INR 527 crore
in FY25 largely due to increase in depreciation
& amortization expense in the quick commerce
business.
Note: To supplement our financial information presented in
accordance with IND AS, we consider certain financial measures
that are not prepared in accordance with IND AS, including Adjusted
Revenue and Adjusted EBITDA. We use these financial measures in
conjunction with IND AS measures as part of overall assessment
of our performance to evaluate the effectiveness of our business
strategies and to communicate with our board of directors
concerning our business and financial performance. We believe
these non-GAAP financial measures provide useful information to
investors about our business and financial performance, enhance
their overall understanding of our past performance and future
prospects, and allow for greater transparency with respect to
metrics used by our management in their financial and operational
decision making. We are presenting these non-GAAP financial
measures to assist our investors and because we believe that
these non-GAAP financial measures provide an additional tool for
investors to use in comparing results of operations of our business
over multiple periods. Information given also includes information
related to material subsidiaries. Non-GAAP measures used by us
are defined below:
a)
Adjusted EBITDA = Consolidated EBITDA (+) share-based
payment expense (-) rental paid for the period pertaining to
‘IND AS 116 leases’
b)
EBITDA = Profit/loss as per financials excluding (i) tax expense
(ii) other income (iii) depreciation and amortization expense
(iv) finance cost and (v) exceptional items

enhancing its effectiveness. Accordingly, the

committees, the chairman and individual directors,
including independent directors.

oversight and decision-making;

Leadership of the chairman and the functioning
of the Board;

Contribution and performance of individual
directors;

Effectiveness of the Board committees in
discharging their responsibilities.
In compliance with Section 149 of the Act, Regulation
17 of the SEBI Listing Regulations and the Company’s
policy for evaluation of the performance of the board
of directors, the performance evaluation of each
independent director was carried out considering
the aspects related to fulfillment of independence
criteria and independence from management.
During the financial year under review, the Company
engaged Nasdaq Corporate Solutions International
Limited (“Nasdaq”), an independent governance
advisory firm, to facilitate the evaluation process. The
evaluation was conducted through a combination of
structured questionnaires, wherein all directors were
requested to complete comprehensive evaluation
questionnaires tailored to capture an objective
overview of the overall performance and one-on-one
interviews with independent directors, providing an
additional layer of qualitative insights and feedback.
The independent and comprehensive evaluation,
consolidated by Nasdaq, assessed the Board’s
overall vitality, offering deep insights into its
leadership mindset, strategic engagement, cultural
ethos, interpersonal dynamics, and governance
practices.
Following a review by the NRC and subsequent
deliberation by the Board, the findings reaffirmed
that the Board and its committees continue to
function effectively and provide appropriate strategic
oversight. It also provided constructive insights
to support the Board’s ongoing focus on strategic

13. Vigil mechanism and whistle
blower policy
The Company is committed to maintain the highest
standards of integrity, transparency, and ethical
conduct.
In alignment with the Section 177 of the Act and
Regulation 22 of the SEBI Listing Regulations, our
Vigil Mechanism and Whistle Blower Policy (“Policy”)
provides a secure, confidential channel for employees,
directors, and stakeholders to report instances of
misconduct, fraud, unethical practices, or violations
of the Code of Conduct. The Policy is available at Link.
This framework ensures direct access to the
chairperson of the Audit Committee and includes
strict safeguards against any form of retaliation
or victimization. All reported concerns undergo
independent investigation, with key findings
presented periodically to the relevant committees.
During the financial year under review, the Company
confirms that no individual was denied access to the
Audit Committee’s chairperson. For the financial
year under review, 9 (nine) reportable matters were
thoroughly investigated and successfully resolved
under the provisions of the Policy.
14. Risk management
The Board has established a Risk Management
Committee (“RMC”) to supervise the Company’s risk
governance framework and verify the effectiveness
of its mitigation strategies.
Integrated directly into core decision-making
processes, this framework enables the systematic
identification, assessment, monitoring, and
mitigation of strategic and operational risks. The
Risk Management Policy details the corporate risk
governance structure—including the “Three Lines
of Defence” model—and defines clear lines of risk
ownership, the said policy is available at Link.
Implementation of this framework is driven by
the Governance, Risk & Compliance team, which
evaluates risks based on its likelihood and impact.
This structured methodology enhances transparency,
supports well-informed decisions, and bolsters the
Company’s capacity to navigate emerging risks and
opportunities.

Financial Statements
Statutory Reports: Board Report
Company Overview


Risks and Concerns
In line with the Enterprise Risk Management (“ERM”)
exercise conducted for Eternal at a consolidated
level, below are the strategic risks that have been
identified and may impact Eternal in the long run.
These risks have been further mapped to the relevant
Environmental, Social, and Governance (“ESG”) pillars
from a sustainability standpoint, with their changing
trends over the past fiscal year tracked closely by
the RMC.
 Competition | Governance
Eternal faces an intensifying competitive landscape
across both established and emerging segments,
which can directly hinder growth, compress operating
margins, and reduce overall platform revenue and
profitability. Key risk factors include capital-fueled
expansions by quick commerce rivals and the entry
of new players. Furthermore, aggressive competitor
tactics—such as eliminating user handling fees
and lowering delivery fee thresholds—exert strong
pricing pressure, triggering risk of customer
migration or heightened monthly inactivity on our B2C
marketplaces.
To protect market share and strengthen our
operational moats, Eternal continues to evolve its
business strategy from rapid expansion to sustained
efficiency. Blinkit has strategically transitioned to an
inventory-owned model to achieve absolute control
over pricing, product availability, and margins,
supplemented by Hyperpure entering the B2C market
as a core seller for fresh and perishable products.
Concurrently, Zomato has scaled user retention
and expanded affordability features by introducing
consumer-centric options like “Healthy Mode” and
budget-conscious “Meals under 250,” while the
District app continues to establish exclusive global
partnerships with international agencies to secure
premium event inventories.
Driven by execution capabilities and strategic agility,
Eternal is dedicated to sustaining its ecosystem
leadership through disciplined cost efficiencies and
differentiated consumer offerings.
 Regulatory Compliances | Governance
As a leading digital platform, Eternal operates under
an evolving legislative and supervisory framework,
where any failure to track, interpret, or communicate
regulatory updates can lead to significant operational
disruptions, heavy financial penalties, or license
cancellations. The compliance landscape has become
increasingly demanding following the notification
of new centralized Labor Codes which introduced
rigid system obligations and social security fund
contributions for our fleet. Additionally, heightened
platform governance requirements—including
explicit data principal consent under Digital Personal
Data Protection Rules, strict country-of-origin
metrology declarations, Bureau of Indian Standards
hallmarking, and enforcement directives from
authorities to eliminate delivery speed claims from
marketing narratives—require continuous, resource-
intensive platform modifications.
To maintain high standards of statutory adherence,
Eternal’s public policy, product, and legal teams
proactively engage with governing authorities to
ensure consistent framework alignments. On the
technology front, our data governance structures
have been enhanced to incorporate robust consent
management systems and transparent country-of-
origin discovery filters, while the entire property
onboarding flow for dark stores has been embedded
with mandatory legal due diligence layers.
Eternal remains committed to fostering an
uncompromised culture of compliance, embedding
transparent regulatory tracking directly into the core
design of our business processes.
 Stakeholder Management | Governance
Eternal’s highly distributed operational model relies
on the seamless alignment of key stakeholders,
making the platform vulnerable to sudden operational
breakthroughs or revenue losses from service
lapses. Labor volatility, underscored by nationwide
or regional gig worker strikes orchestrated by
platform unions over social security structures,
presents an immediate threat to last-mile fulfillment
continuity and order volumes. Similarly, supply chain
constraints across our B2B verticals—driven by
intensive competitive hiring and high turnover among
warehouse personnel seeking aggressive payout
structures—can restrict our ability to reliably fulfill
demand during peak business windows.  To mitigate
friction and fortify stakeholder relationships, our
city and central teams have established responsive
communication protocols and structured grievance
channels. During high-demand and festive
57
Financial Statements
Statutory Reports: Board Report
Company Overview


windows, we actively calibrate our rate cards and
performance incentives to reward partners fairly,
ensuring continuous fleet availability and safe
operations through local coordination. Hyperpure is
actively aligning its payout structures with market
dynamics to match competitive frequencies and
has collaborated with third-party vendors to extend
enhanced social security scheme onboarding and
visibility to warehouse personnel. Furthermore, our
commitment to systemic equity is reinforced through
large-scale platforms, such as hosting the inclusive
growth conference in New Delhi to advance collective
partner well-being.
Recognizing that our partners form the vital
infrastructure of our business, Eternal is dedicated
to cultivating a fair, rewarding, and deeply sustainable
ecosystem for all participants.
 Macro-Economics | Social & Governance
Fluctuations in localized and global economic
conditions, alongside broader geopolitical volatility,
can severely restrict consumer discretionary
spending and compress corporate operating
margins. Escalations in international conflicts pose
severe logistics constraints, leading to dual-impact
crises such as global energy transit bottlenecks
that simultaneously inflate platform delivery costs
and disrupt the fuel and LPG dependencies of our
restaurant partners. Such structural shortages
directly manifest as revenue bottlenecks, causing
impact in order volumes and restricting restaurant
supply capabilities across our core B2C and B2B
segments.  Eternal leverages its multi-vertical
business architecture to reduce dependency on any
single market segment or price point, providing a
natural cushion against macro-level discretionary
contractions. To insulate our fleet from volatile crude
oil metrics, Zomato and Blinkit have accelerated their
strategic transition to EV-based logistics, optimizing
local routes and enhancing fleet efficiency. Our
procurement teams have strengthened supply chain
resilience by diversifying our supplier base and
building robust inventory reserves, while proactively
planning for alternative energy options to protect
our restaurant partners during macro bottlenecks.
Through continuous macroeconomic monitoring
and structured financial discipline, Eternal remains
agile, maintaining its capacity to deliver long-term
stakeholder value despite global uncertainties.
 Brand / Reputation | Governance
In an environment heavily driven by digital interactions,
Eternal faces persistent brand exposure from adverse
publicity, social media perception pressures, and
ineffective customer experiences during complex
operations. Public narratives can shift rapidly due to
political criticism regarding gig worker welfare, public
protests, or administrative and crowd management
bottlenecks during major co-produced ticketing
events. Such incidents can inflate negative social
media mentions, trigger elevated refund requests,
and place sudden stress on platform support
infrastructures, presenting a challenge to long-term
user retention and brand loyalty.  To safeguard brand
health and maintain public trust, Eternal utilizes
advanced analytics tools to pre-emptively monitor
social narratives and deploy immediate corporate
communications. We consistently validate our
commitment to our fleet through wide-ranging
transparency initiatives regarding partner earnings
and have rolled out multi-city road safety campaigns
in coordination with local authorities. For our going-
out verticals, platform features have been upgraded
to include consumer-friendly safeguards such as a
“Refund Guarantee Fee” for flexible cancellations
and upfront payment capabilities to secure high-
demand inventory smoothly.  Guided by long-term
transparency and a responsible marketing approach,
Eternal continues to reinforce consumer trust and
secure its brand equity across all touchpoints.
 Black Swan Disruption | Governance
Unforeseen, large-scale catastrophic events—
including severe geopolitical conflicts, regional trade
or tariff disruptions, public health emergencies, and
localized security incidents—pose fundamental risks to
consumer behavior and financial stability. Given that a
substantial concentration of group Gross Merchandise
Value (GMV) is anchored within top-tier urban metros,
any localized mobility restriction, trade deadlock,
or supply shock can trigger severe operational and
financial vulnerabilities across our consolidated
network.  To ensure uninterrupted business continuity
under adverse conditions, Eternal has systematically
decentralized its operational footprint and established
a geographically distributed supply architecture. We
intentionally source critical inventory and materials
from a diversified pool of independent vendors across
separate regions, eliminating over-reliance on singular
58
Financial Statements
Statutory Reports: Board Report
Company Overview


supply vectors. Furthermore, the Company is actively
embedding advanced technological solutions—
including deeper logistically focused automated
systems—to systematically reduce platform
dependency on highly volatile human and energy
variables.  By prioritizing deep system resilience
and predictive planning, Eternal stands prepared
to navigate complex, large-scale disruptions while
safeguarding core operational integrity.

16. Internal financial controls and
their adequacy
As an integral component of the Company’s risk
and governance framework, internal financial
controls mitigate financial and operational risks to
ensure orderly and efficient business conduct. This
framework secures adherence to corporate policies,
safeguards assets, prevents and detects fraud,
maintains the accuracy of accounting records, and
facilitates the timely preparation of reliable financial
statements.
Commensurate with the scale of its operations, the
Company maintains a robust internal control system
over financial reporting. This framework ensures
that all transactions are duly authorized, recorded,
and reported in compliance with the applicable
accounting standards. Key controls have been
thoroughly documented, automated where feasible,
and embedded directly into core business processes.
Oversight and assurance regarding control
effectiveness are delivered to the Board through
three structured lines of defense:

Management Oversight: Maintained through
ongoing business reviews and self-assessments.

Risk Compliance: Driven by continuous
monitoring from the Governance, Risk, and
Compliance function.

Independent Audits: Verified through
comprehensive design and operational testing
by the Statutory and Secretarial Auditors.
The Company confirms that its internal financial
controls were adequate and operating effectively
throughout the financial year under review. This
evaluation is further validated by the Statutory
Auditors, whose independent testing revealed no
material weaknesses or significant deficiencies.

19. Disclosure under the Sexual
Harassment of Women at Workplace
(Prevention, Prohibition and
Redressal) Act, 2013
The Company maintains a zero-tolerance approach
towards sexual harassment and is committed to
provide a safe, respectful, and inclusive workplace.
In compliance with the Sexual Harassment of Women
at Workplace (Prevention, Prohibition and Redressal)
Act, 2013 (“POSH Act”) and the rules thereunder,
the Company has adopted a Prevention of Sexual
Harassment (“POSH”) policy.
The POSH policy is inclusive and gender-neutral,
covering employees across genders, including those
who identify as members of the LGBTQI+ community.
The Company has established a robust framework for
reporting and addressing complaints while ensuring
confidentiality.
The POSH policy is available at Link.
To implement the POSH policy, the Company has
constituted an Internal Complaints Committee (“ICC”)
in accordance with the Section 4 of the POSH Act. The
ICC comprises members with relevant and diverse
experiences, and is responsible for investigating
complaints, ensuring fair and impartial resolution,
and promoting awareness through training and
sensitization initiatives designed to prevent
harassment and promote a culture of dignity and
inclusion.

Technology absorption
Eternal continuously explores and deploys
technological, product, and service innovations
across its business portfolio. Notable recent
advancements in financial FY26 include:

Nugget: Originally introduced in FY25, Nugget is
an AI-native customer support platform designed
to autonomously manage and resolve customer
grievances. Its deployment has significantly
accelerated query resolution times, enabled
highly personalized support, and elevated overall
customer satisfaction while driving substantial
cost efficiencies. Furthermore, Nugget is offered
as a B2B solution, allowing external businesses to
effortlessly upgrade their own customer support
operations.

Healthy Mode: Launched on the Zomato app in
FY26, “Healthy Mode” empowers users to make
highly informed dietary choices. Rather than
focusing solely on calories, this feature assigns
a comprehensive “Healthy Score”—ranging from
‘Low’ to ‘Super’—to every dish based on essential
nutritional elements such as proteins, complex
carbohydrates, fiber, and micronutrients. Such
information is derived from restaurants’ item
description data and food images, which is
analysed via our proprietary AI-model.

Statutory Reports: Board Report
Company Overview



Ambulance Service: Enabled on the Blinkit app,
with an aim to bring reliable emergency support
closer to the consumers, addressing a wide range
of emergencies including cardiac arrest, seizure,
roadside accident as well as OPD (Outpatient
Department) cases. The service is equipped
with essential life-saving equipment, including
stretcher, scoop, wheelchair, vital-monitoring
devices, oxygen cylinder, AED (Automated
External Defibrillator), a suction machine, and
essential medicine and fluid.

Parental Controls Feature: Blinkit being the first
quick commerce platform, where consumers
can set a 6-digit PIN to hide sensitive items from
certain product categories, such that younger
age groups can browse the app without seeing
age inappropriate products. In case there are any

ANNEXURE – II
REPORT ON CSR ACTIVITIES
Brief outline on CSR policy of the Company
The Company’s CSR policy reflects its commitment to creating sustainable value for society while conducting
its business in a responsible, ethical and environmentally conscious manner. The policy is founded on the belief
that businesses have an important role to play in contributing towards inclusive and sustainable development
by creating a positive impact on the communities and the environment around their areas of operation.
The Company voluntarily undertakes initiatives with the objective of supporting programmes that create
meaningful, measurable and long-term social impact. It further enables the Company to undertake projects
across the areas including initiatives relating to healthcare, education, livelihood enhancement, environmental
sustainability, hunger eradication, disaster relief and other activities that address evolving societal needs.
The Company believes that its CSR initiatives will be implemented in a transparent, accountable and efficient
manner, with appropriate oversight and periodic review to ensure effective utilisation of resources and
achievement of the intended outcomes.
The CSR policy also sets out the roles and responsibilities of the Board and the CSR Committee in providing
strategic direction and oversight, including the approval, monitoring and review of CSR projects and expenditure,
if any. During the financial year under review, there was no change in the CSR policy of the Company. The CSR
policy is available on the Company’s website.

We have conducted the Secretarial Audit of the
compliance of applicable statutory provisions
and the adherence to good corporate governance
practices by Eternal Limited (formerly known as
Zomato Limited) (hereinafter called “the Company”).
Secretarial Audit was conducted in a manner that
provided us a reasonable basis for evaluating the
corporate conducts/ statutory compliances and
expressing our opinion thereon.
Based on our verification of the Company’s books,
papers, minute books, forms and returns filed and
other records maintained by the Company and also
the information provided by the Company, its officers,
agents and authorized representatives during the
conduct of secretarial audit, we hereby report that in
our opinion, the Company has, during the audit period
covering the financial year ended on March 31, 2026
(“Audit Period”/ “period under review”) complied
with the statutory provisions listed hereunder and
also that the Company has proper Board-processes
and compliance-mechanism in place to the extent,
in the manner and subject to the reporting made
hereinafter.
We have examined the books, papers, minute books,
forms and returns filed and other records maintained
by the Company for the financial year ended on March
31, 2026 according to the provisions of:
(i)
The Companies Act, 2013 (‘the Act’) and the rules
made thereunder;
(ii)	 The Securities Contracts (Regulation) Act, 1956
(‘SCRA’) and the rules made thereunder;
(iii)	 The Depositories Act, 1996 and the Regulations
and Bye-laws framed thereunder to the extent of
Regulation 76 and 74(5) of SEBI (Depositories and
Participants) Regulations, 2018;
(iv)	 Foreign Exchange Management Act, 1999 and
the rules and regulations made thereunder to the
extent of Foreign Direct Investment, Overseas Direct
Investment and External Commercial Borrowings to
the extent applicable;
(v)	 The following Regulations and Guidelines
prescribed under the Securities and Exchange Board
of India Act, 1992 (‘SEBI Act’):-
(a)	 The Securities and Exchange Board of India
(Substantial Acquisition of Shares and Takeovers)
Regulations, 2011;
(b)	 The Securities and Exchange Board of India
(Prohibition of Insider Trading) Regulations, 2015;
(c)	 The Securities and Exchange Board of India (Issue
of Capital and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations,
2018 to the extent applicable;
(d)	 The Securities and Exchange Board of India
(Share Based Employee Benefits and Sweat Equity)
Regulations, 2021 to the extent applicable;
(e)	 The Securities and Exchange Board of India
(Issue and Listing of Non-Convertible Securities)
Regulations, 2021; Not applicable to the Company
during the Audit Period
(f)	 The Securities and Exchange Board of India
(Registrars to an Issue and Share Transfer Agents)
Regulations, 2025 regarding the Companies Act and
dealing with client to the extent of securities issued;
(g)	 The Securities and Exchange Board of India
(Delisting of Equity Shares) Regulations, 2021; Not
applicable to the Company during the Audit Period

Auditor’s responsibility
Based on audit, our responsibility is to express an
opinion on the compliance with the applicable laws
and maintenance of records by the Company. We
conducted our audit in accordance with the auditing
standards CSAS 1 to CSAS 4 (“CSAS”) prescribed by
the Institute of Company Secretaries of India (“ICSI”).
These standards require that the auditor complies
with statutory and regulatory requirements and
plans and performs the audit to obtain reasonable
assurance about compliance with applicable laws and
maintenance of records. Our report of even date is to
be read along with this letter.
1.
Maintenance of secretarial record is the
responsibility of the management of the Company.
Our responsibility is to express an opinion on these
secretarial records based on our audit.
2.	 We have followed the audit practices and
processes as were appropriate to obtain reasonable
assurance about the correctness of the contents of
the secretarial records. The verification was done on
the random test basis to ensure that correct facts
are reflected in secretarial records. We believe that
the processes and practices, we followed provide a
reasonable basis for our opinion.
3.
We have not verified the correctness and
appropriateness of financial records and Books of
Accounts of the Company and for which we relied on
the report of statutory auditor.
4.
Wherever required, we have obtained the
Management representation about the compliance of
laws, rules and regulations and happening of events
etc.
5.	 The compliance of the provisions of Corporate and
other applicable laws, rules, regulations, standards is
the responsibility of management. Our examination
was limited to the verification of procedures on
random test basis.
6.	 The Secretarial Audit Report is neither an
assurance as to the future viability of the company
nor of the efficacy or effectiveness with which
the management has conducted the affairs of the
Company.

*Eternal Limited (formerly known as Zomato Limited), on a standalone basis, has employees working out of 4 directly leased offices
as on 31st March 2026 which is reflected in the table above. In addition, our employees work out of 48 co-working spaces in India
and the UAE. We also have 14 single seater non-operational offices with no employees. The reporting boundary for all financial,
social and governance disclosures in this report, unless otherwise stated, includes all types of offices.
Our environmental disclosures are based on the principles of operational control. These disclosures have been apportioned and
extrapolated based on data availability, and are further discussed topic-wise as below:
Energy: Our disclosures include energy from fuel used in owned vehicles, Diesel Generator (DG) sets and purchased electricity for
directly leased offices where we have established operational control. Energy from electricity consumed across our offices outside
our operational control is placed within our value chain and will be taken into account in our value chain disclosures.
Greenhouse Gas (GHG) emissions: Our Scope 1 GHG emissions are limited to those generated by refilling of gas in owned ACs
and fire extinguishers, fuel used in owned vehicles and directly leased DG sets. Emissions from electricity consumption in directly
leased offices where we have established operational control are reported in Scope 2 of GHG emissions.
Water: Our boundary for water withdrawal, consumption and discharge is restricted to directly leased offices where we have
established operational control, following the principle of operational control in accordance with Carbon Disclosure Project (CDP)
and GHG Protocol guidance.
Waste: The reporting boundary for our waste disclosures includes directly leased offices where we have established operational
control and procured waste management services ourselves.
These environmental disclosures have been apportioned and extrapolated based on data availability.
Details on the inclusions within above topics are mentioned under Section C, Principle 6 of this report.

c. 	 A brief on types of customers*
For the purpose of this BRSR disclosure, we have three types of customers as defined below:
1. 	 End-users of our platforms- End-users are customers who use our platforms to search and
discover restaurants, read and share reviews, view and upload photos, order food, book a table,
discover and transact across going out experiences.
2. 	 Restaurant partners- We provide restaurant partners with industry-specific technological tools
which enable them to engage and acquire customers to grow their business while also providing
a reliable and efficient last-mile delivery service.
3. 	 Business partners- These include other partners onboarded on our platform to engage and
acquire customers. We also provide business partners technological tools that enable customer
discovery, participation in going-out experiences by offering curated offers and discounts and
to manage certain parts of their business operations.

customers only under limited circumstances. For the purpose of this BRSR disclosure, the definition
of customers includes end-users, restaurant and business partners while delivery partners have been
considered as value chain partners.
These customers interact with us via our Zomato Food Ordering and Delivery app, Zomato Dining Partner
app, Zomato Restaurant Partner app, District app and other business dashboards/ websites.

26. Overview of the entity’s material responsible business conduct issues
Please indicate material responsible business conduct and sustainability issues pertaining to
environmental and social matters that present a risk or an opportunity to your business, rationale for
identifying the same, approach to adapt or mitigate the risk along-with its financial implications, as per
the following format:
In FY26, we conducted a double materiality assessment with an independent external organization to
re-assess our sustainability priorities in line with evolving business needs and stakeholder expectations.
The assessment evaluated sustainability issues through the dual lens of impact and financial materiality,
enabling us to understand both the impact our business has on people and the environment, as well as the
potential implications of sustainability-related issues on our business performance and long-term value
creation. The outcome of the exercise for Eternal Limited on a standalone basis is reflected in the list of
material topics below organized under Environment, Social and Governance categories.
S.
No.
Material
identified
issue
Indicate
whether
risk or

(R/O)
Rationale for

In case of risk, approach to adapt or

Financial implications
of the risk or
opportunity
(Indicate positive or

1.
Climate action
and circular
economy
Risk
Climate action and
circular economy
are critical to the
business’s long-
term growth and
resilience. Climate
change effects,
such as rising
temperature,
changing
precipitation
patterns and
extreme weather
events, pose a
risk to business
operations,
impacting last-mile
food deliveries
and customer
dining experience.
Efficient use of
energy, water and
other resources
promotes resource
circularity, reduces
waste and helps
mitigate risks
associated with
resource scarcity.
	 Periodic Climate Risk Assessment-
In FY24, we undertook a climate risk
assessment exercise for our food
ordering and delivery business. We
assessed the impact of physical climate
risks such as cyclones, storms, heavy
precipitation, increasing/ decreasing
temperature, flooding and heat/ cold
waves and transitional risks across our
top 15 cities under IPCC RCP 2.6 and
RCP 8.5 scenarios*. The outcome of this
exercise has been integrated into Eternal
Limited’s Enterprise Risk Management
(ERM) process. We have also published a
TCFD report on Eternal’s Food Ordering
and Delivery Business in the
Impact section of Eternal’s website.
	 Transition to EV-based food deliveries-
Our key climate risk mitigation action is to
support delivery partners in transitioning
to EV bikes for undertaking food
deliveries. To achieve this, we generate
awareness about EVs among delivery
partners, collaborate with EV ecosystem
players to secure access for delivery
partners to EVs on rent and advocate for
better EV products, infrastructure and
financing. In FY26, total EV-based food
deliveries increased by 50% compared to
the previous year, totaling 132.3 million
orders. This represented 13.5% of total
FY26 food delivery orders.
For more details, please refer to
Principle 6, Essential indicator 8
Negative implication-
Ignoring climate
change risks and
failure to adopt circular
economy practices
can result in increased
operational and supply
chain costs, regulatory
fines, declining
customer preference
and reputational
damage, resulting
in negative financial
impact.

Climate action
and circular
economy
Risk
	 Climate adaptation support for Delivery
partners- We expanded our Automatic
Weather Station (AWS) network to
1,150 devices across 100 cities to guide
business decisions, predict weather
conditions and communicate them to
delivery partners. Additionally, a network
of merchant outlets was made available
as rest points for delivery partners,
enabling access to basic amenities such
as drinking water, rest areas, washrooms
and mobile charging facilities during
their workday. The network was scaled to
3,395 locations in FY26. We also provide
access to custom-made and off-the-
shelf apparel that protects partners from
the unpredictability of changing climate,
including raincoats and cooling vests.
In FY26, 3,000 cooling vest jackets
were provided under a pilot to delivery
partners to assess their effectiveness
in reducing heat stress and to provide
design feedback to the manufacturer.
	 Carbon market instruments- We
maintained net GHG emissions from our
operations (classified as Scope 1 & Scope
2) at zero by procuring verified carbon
removal offsets equivalent to 100% of
our Scope 1 emissions and International
Renewable Energy Certificates (IRECs)
for Scope 2 emissions.
	 Circular economy- Waste management
is crucial to reducing the environmental
impact of our value chain operations.
Across our own offices, we have
implemented responsible waste disposal
and water usage initiatives. For the
food delivery value chain we run several
voluntary initiatives:
	 Waste reduction- ‘Don’t send cutlery’
and the ‘Food Rescue’ initiatives are
two key features within the Zomato
app that help avoid the waste from
food delivery operations. Allowing
users to opt out of receiving cutlery
with their order has helped avoid
~3,000 MT of cutlery waste over 4
years. The Food Rescue feature
enables cancelled but untampered
food orders to be made available
to nearby customers at discounted
prices, helping reduce food wastage.
Since launch in FY25, Zomato’s
Food Rescue feature has helped
prevent over 7.5 lakh kg of food
waste by diverting cancelled orders
to interested buyers (assuming each
order weighs approximately 400g).

	 Sustainable packaging-  We host

to sustainable packaging solutions

that adopt sustainable alternatives to

2.
Human capital
development
Opportunity
& Risk
Excellence in
human capital
development can
result in strong
employer branding
and a better culture
of innovation.
Failure to prioritize
human capital
development,
including
attracting and
retaining a diverse
talent pool and
supporting
upskilling,
can constrain
innovation and
limit the company’s
ability to respond
to evolving market
demands.
	 Talent discovery- We follow a non-
traditional approach to talent discovery
that depends mainly on employee
referrals which, in our experience,
improves the quality of hires. We also
leverage traditional channels such as
networking sites, job portals, campus
placements and partnerships with
external organizations to tap into diverse
pools of talent.
	 Championing a culture of learning
by doing- To support fast-paced
innovation, Eternal’s culture emphasizes
ownership and autonomy over technical
qualifications. The organization actively
supports the movement of people into
roles where they may not have prior
exposure but have the willingness and
commitment to learn. Employees are
encouraged to demonstrate a founders’
mindset and take ownership of problems
well outside their department or comfort
zone. We reward employees for both
positive outcomes and failures. We
also invest in learning and development
programs - both offline and online - to
enable employees to upskill for evolving
business requirements.
Positive and negative
implication-
Strong performance
in human capital
development results
in better talent and
lower hiring costs.
Gaps in human capital
development can lead
to higher recruitment
and training costs,
while limited workforce
diversity may hinder
ability to respond
to evolving market
demands.

Human capital

Opportunity
& Risk
	 Fostering Inclusion- Diversity is
supported by Eternal’s culture. The
organization operates with a flat, non-
hierarchical structure that generates
a lot of leadership opportunities for
younger people and those from diverse
groups. Our Employee Resource
Groups (ERGs) and online communities
organized around identity, life stage
and interest, help foster belonging and
engagement among diverse groups
of employees. Our Equal opportunity,
diversity and inclusion policy and
associated SoPs, ensure fair, non-
discriminatory treatment for all
employees.
By endorsing global standards such
as the UN Women’s Empowerment
Principles (UN WEPs) and the UN
Standards of Conduct for Business
Tackling Discrimination against
LGBTQIA+ people, we affirm our
dedication to fostering an inclusive
workplace.
	 Open channels for employee
communication- Eternal is committed
to maintaining transparent and
accessible channels for employees to
communicate with each other and with
leadership. We encourage employee
communication and support grievance
redressal via a variety of offline and
online channels such as townhalls,
slack, email and surveys. All leaders
maintain an open-door policy. Formal
processes are in place to address
concerns related to sexual harassment,
whistleblower complaints, workplace
conduct and human rights issues in a
fair, confidential and timely manner.
	 Continuous feedback and 360-degree
performance appraisal- Eternal follows
a 360-degree performance appraisal
method that provides a comprehensive
view of an employee’s strengths,
weaknesses and blind spots, enabling
targeted human capital development
interventions. While formal
performance appraisals are conducted
annually, managers and employees are
encouraged to share feedback more
frequently to facilitate faster learning,
course correction and recognition.

3.
Health, safety,
and dignity
for all
Risk
Ensuring a safe and
respectful working
environment
is core to how
we operate.
Inadequate health
& safety measures
can lead to injuries
or casualties
affecting well-
being. This may
also lead to higher
attrition and loss
of stakeholder
trust. Failure to
uphold human
rights, including
health & safety,
can further expose
the company to
regulatory risks,
with implications
for business
continuity.
	 Employees and workers- Our
occupational health & safety framework
is supported through a comprehensive
policy and robust workplace safety
systems. These include mandatory
training for employees and workers
on health & safety, fire detection
& fire fighting mechanisms, safety
signage and the Safety Reporting
System (SRS) for proactive incident
reporting and resolution. We have
secured ISO 45001:2018 certification
for the Occupational Health and Safety
Management systems in our Gurugram
and Bengaluru leased offices. Please
refer to Principle 3, Essential indicator 10
for more details.
	 Delivery partners
	 Road safety awareness and training-
All delivery partners are required to
complete a mandatory road safety
awareness module at the time of
onboarding. This is supplemented by
periodic health & safety programs
conducted through both online and
offline formats. Delivery partners
also receive educational content and
videos on safe riding practices under
varied weather conditions such as rain,
heat and fog. In addition, we regularly
organize safety camps in collaboration
with local traffic authorities and
have partnered with the Ministry of
Road Transport and Highways for the
national campaign ‘Sadak Suraksha
Abhiyan’ promoting responsible road
behavior among delivery partners and
encouraging customers not to rush
them.
	 SOS service- An SOS service,
accessible via the delivery partner
app, is available to provide immediate
support to all delivery partners in
case of emergencies, including
accidents, vehicle breakdowns, etc.
The SOS service is set up to provide
free ambulance to partners in 900+
cities, depending on the emergency.
Ambulances are positioned to reach
partners within 20 minutes.
	 Insurance coverage- Eternal
voluntarily provides accidental and
medical insurance coverage, inclusive
of disability and death benefits for all
active delivery partners. The premium
for this insurance is borne entirely by
Eternal.
Negative implication-
Inadequate health
& safety measures
across operations can
increase the risk of
work-related injuries or
casualties, adversely
affecting well-being.
Failure to uphold
human rights, including
the right to safe
working conditions,
may lead to business
disruptions, regulatory
penalties, legal
liabilities and cause
reputational damage,
resulting in negative
financial impact.

Health, safety,
and dignity
for all
Risk
	 No time-based pressure- Delivery
partners are only shown the distance
to the customer’s location and are
not shown the delivery time promised
to customers, helping reduce time-
based pressure while on the road.
	 Rest points- A network of merchant
outlets is available as rest points for
delivery partners, enabling access
to basic amenities such as drinking
water, rest areas, washrooms and
mobile charging facilities. The
network was scaled to 3,395 locations
in FY26.
	 Black zone reporting- Delivery
partners can report unsafe or difficult-
to-navigate areas through the delivery
partner app. More than 270 black
zones were identified as on 31st March
2026, with such locations marked as
unserviceable, where required, to
enhance safety.
	 Upholding human rights
	 Zero tolerance- Eternal has a strict
zero-tolerance policy for any form
of discrimination or harassment
(including sexual harassment). The
policy applies to all internal and
external stakeholders, including
delivery partners, restaurant
partners, customers and Eternal
employees and workers.
	 Third party human rights assessment
and regular surveys- In FY26, Human
Rights Assessment was carried out by
an independent third party consultant
which included a comprehensive
evaluation of the organization’s
policies, practices and procedures
and no material gaps were identified
during the assessment. We also
conducted a large-scale pan-India
Delivery Partner Wellbeing survey that
covered topics of health, safety and
dignity.
	 Accessibility for Persons with
Disabilities (PwD)- We conduct
periodic assessments for our directly
leased offices and all new facilities
under our direct lease have been
evaluated for accessibility standards
outlined in the RPwD Act, 2016.
Please refer to our Human rights policy
and Health and safety policy for more
details.

4.
Innovation
Opportunity Continuous
innovation
across platform
technology, product
and services
enhances customer
experience,
improves
operational
efficiency, enables
discovery of
new business
opportunities and
supports Eternal’s
competitive
positioning and
long-term growth.
Eternal is constantly testing and launching
innovations in technology, product and
service offerings across its businesses.
For the standalone entity, in FY26, some
notable, recent innovations include:
	 Nugget- Eternal introduced Nugget in
FY25 as an AI-native customer support
platform that autonomously resolves
customer grievances delivering faster
resolutions, personalized support, higher
customer satisfaction and significant
cost savings. Since then, Nugget has
evolved beyond support into building
conversational AI experiences for
enterprises across different sectors,
through voice and non-voice agents that
work together to replicate full contact
centre operations. Nugget today powers
over 15 million automated interactions
every month.
At Eternal, we have deployed AI across
our applications to enhance platform
experience.
	 Healthy mode- To capture the growing
demand for healthier meals, Eternal
launched “Healthy mode” on the
Zomato app in top 7 cities in FY26. With
this mode, end users  see a curation
of  restaurants along with complete
nutritional information, i.e. macros,
ingredient-level description and
customization filters. Since its launch,
Healthy Mode has helped customers
make more informed and healthier food
choices, generating ~8.5 million orders
in FY26.
Positive implication-
Innovations in
technology, product
and service enhance
platform experience
for our customers
and delivery
partners- providing
us a competitive edge
while contributing to
long-term financial
performance.

Innovation
Opportunity
	 Chef’s table experience- A thoughtfully
curated multi-course dining-at-
home experience from some of the
finest restaurants, delivered directly
to customers’ homes. Each menu
is designed to bring the restaurant
experience home, with premium course-
wise packaging for a ‘Meal for 2’. The
program is live in Delhi NCR, Mumbai and
Bengaluru across 250+ restaurant brands
and 650+ outlets.
	 Zomato for Enterprise (ZFE)- ZFE is
a platform designed for companies to
solve food expense management. A lot
of Zomato orders placed by corporate
employees are business-related and need
to be reimbursed by their employer via
processes that are often, cumbersome
and time-consuming. With ZFE,
employees can simply bill their business
orders directly to their employer, without
having to pay, adding convenience
for employees and transparency for
companies. Over 350 companies have
adopted ZFE.
	 Food on Train- Long-distance Indian rail
carries enormous passenger volumes,
yet onboard catering has limited choices
and inconsistent quality. Zomato’s Food
on Train service fixes this through its
IRCTC partnership: customers order
on the app, enter their PNR, and pick a
station along the route, and Zomato’s
station walkers hand the order over right
at the coach gate. Delivering this service
meant building real-time train tracking
and a dedicated layer of walker logistics
synced to each station halt. Zomato
delivers at 250+ stations, pan-India.

Stakeholder
value creation
Opportunity As a platform-
led business,
creating shared
value across
our ecosystem
of restaurant
partners, delivery
partners,
suppliers,
customers and
business partners,
is integral to our
long-term growth
and resilience. By
enabling income
generation

and promoting
responsible
sourcing practices,
we strengthen the

ecosystem while
