Below are examples of how we create value
for stakeholders:
	 Restaurant partners
In FY26, Eternal’s food ordering and
delivery business generated earnings
through delivery for 5 lakh+ restaurant
partners, 2.45 lakh+ of which are
delivery-only restaurants
	 Restaurant Services Hub- In 2024,
we launched a voluntary initiative
that supports restaurants in finding
vendors for special equipment
(such as induction stoves), staffing,
securing FSSAI licenses and
conducting hygiene audits. In
FY26, 37,000 restaurants availed
services via the hub.
	 Delivery partners
In FY26, Eternal’s food ordering and
delivery business generated earnings
through delivery for over 18.5 lakh unique
individuals. Beyond generating earnings,
we create value via special initiatives
	 Financial inclusion and social
security for delivery partners-
Delivery partners associated with
Eternal have access to free-of-cost
medical and accidental private
insurance, including OPD treatment,
hospitalization cover and loss of pay
support. We also organize drives
to enrol partners into relevant
government schemes and insurance
programs, support them in tax filing
and in accessing financial services
and products such as fixed and
recurring deposits and pension
schemes via the delivery partner app.
For more details, please refer Principle 4,
Leadership indicator 2
	 Women delivery partner initiatives-
Eternal has targeted initiatives to
support women’s participation in
last-mile delivery beyond the flexibility
provided by the platform to all
partners. These include the option to
undertake short-distance deliveries
only, period rest days, additional
safety measures, maternity benefits
and Women’s Riding Centers focused
on 2-wheeler and digital literacy
training in select cities. As a result,
women’s participation in Zomato’s
delivery partner fleet grew to over
3,500 in March 2026.
For more details, please refer Principle 4,
Leadership indicator 3
Positive implication-
Investments in
strengthening
livelihoods,
expanding economic
opportunities, enabling
financial inclusion
and integration of
responsible sourcing
practices contributes
to higher stakeholder
retention, improved
service quality and
greater operational
stability, enhancing the
company’s financial
performance.

this program, we assess suppliers
on key ESG parameters, upskill
them on reporting, performance
and implement a standardized ESG
screening framework. The program
aims to embed sustainability
considerations into procurement
decisions while recognizing suppliers
that align with mutual sustainability
values and sourcing expectations.
For more details, please refer Principle 2,
Essential indicator 2
	 Communities

the country. Feeding India supports
meals for 1.4 lakh+ children daily and
has served over 290 million meals since
inception till FY26.

6.
Data privacy,
cybersecurity
and
responsible AI
Risk
Inadequate
cybersecurity,
data privacy and
AI governance
measures may
expose the
organization to
various cyber
threats/ incidents,
including
ransomware
attacks, phishing
scams, Denial-
of-Service (DoS)
attacks, and
others, which
may result in data
breaches, service
disruptions,
and the loss of
confidential or
sensitive data of
key stakeholders,
including
Personally
Identifiable
Information (PII)
which may lead to
loss of stakeholder
trust.
	 Information security policy and
framework- Eternal has formulated
an information security policy and
framework to protect Eternal’s data
and information from unauthorized
access and external threats. A dedicated
information security team is responsible
for monitoring the effectiveness of our
cybersecurity policies and systems.
	 ISO Certification- Eternal is committed
to adhering to global best practices
for data protection and continues to
hold ISO  27001:2022 certification for
the information security management
systems. These practices include annual
mandatory cybersecurity awareness for
all employees.
	 Periodic assessment- Eternal
prioritizes continuous improvement in
cybersecurity via thorough and periodic
review mechanisms. These include
independent assessments such as
audits, Vulnerability Assessment and
Penetration Testing (VAPT) evaluations,
third party reviews and bug bounty
programs. These initiatives help us  stay
vigilant and proactive in safeguarding our
systems and data against evolving cyber
threats.
	 Investment in technologies- We invest
in advanced cybersecurity and data
protection technologies, reinforcing
the control framework to mitigate
existing and emerging risks effectively.
We conduct regular company-wide
awareness sessions to educate and
empower all employees with the latest
best practices, fostering a culture of
vigilance and resilience across our
organization.
	 Responsible AI governance - We are
committed to the responsible and ethical
use of artificial intelligence across our
business. User privacy remains a priority,
with all data used for AI development
and deployment maintained in secure,
access-controlled environments
and managed in line with ethical data
practices. Real-time monitoring
protocols track model performance,
validate accuracy, and proactively
detect and address algorithmic bias or
data drift. AI-generated responses and
outputs are clearly labelled so users know
when they are interacting with artificial
intelligence. We also conduct employee
training and awareness initiatives to help
them understand the ethical, legal, and
safety considerations associated with AI.
Negative implication-
Ineffective
cybersecurity and AI
governance measures
may lead to data
breaches, service
disruptions, and the
loss of confidential
or sensitive data
including PII of key
stakeholders. This
may result in loss of
stakeholder trust,
regulatory fines, legal
liabilities, diminished
market reputation
and negative financial
implications.

Brand and

Opportunity
& Risk
Eternal’s corporate
and consumer
brands present
an enormous
opportunity for
creating positive
financial value, if
they are managed
well with adequate
safeguards.
Reputational
risks arising from
adverse publicity,
misinformation,
or misleading
communication
across platforms
may negatively
impact stakeholder
trust and brand
perception.
Inconsistent
service quality and
weak grievance
resolution may
result in revenue
loss and declining
brand loyalty for
Eternal.
	 Communicating responsibly- All
our marketing and advertising
communications are bound by our Code
of Conduct and internal guidelines
to ensure communication with
stakeholders is ethical, transparent,
accurate, inclusive and mindful of
diverse audiences. We carefully review
each piece of communication issued by
our company to internal and external
stakeholders to ensure it is  truthful,
accurate, appropriate and compliant with
applicable brand guidelines, company
policies, laws and regulations.
	 Multi-channel feedback collection on
services supporting timely response-
We have strong, multi-channel
feedback collection mechanisms in
place to maintain high levels of service
quality to diverse groups of platform
users. Apart from teams of restaurant
account managers and fleet operations
managers, who manage relationships
in every city we operate in, we also have
dedicated central customer, restaurant
and delivery partner support teams
that track offline and online channels
to ensure timely query and grievance
resolution. In FY26, we also deployed
AI towards faster resolution of queries
and complaints from end users, delivery
partners and restaurant partners. We
regularly collect ratings from these
groups via the apps to continuously
enhance their platform experience.
Collated feedback is converted into
actions that can enhance brand loyalty.
For more details please refer to Principle
9, Leadership indicator 4.
Please refer to the ‘Advertising’ section
under our Code of Conduct for Employees
for more details.
Positive and negative
implication-
Being perceived as
a fair, responsible,
transparent and
dependable brand
helps us attract and
retain high quality,
premium customers
and reliable business
partners. Strong brand
and reputation helps
to lower business
costs and improves
the quality and pace
of innovation, helping
Eternal maintain its
competitive edge in
the market while also
making a positive
contribution to society
and the planet.  Clear
and transparent
communication
reinforces trust and
provides support for
a long-term stable
financial performance.
Negative customer
feedback that remains
unaddressed and poor
quality of service
can weaken brand
trust and increase
marketing costs we will
need to incur to attract
new customers.

training sessions.
	 Governance Oversight- The company

Positive and negative

and responsible
business practices
ensure enhanced and
structured decision-
making, improved
risk management,
a competitive edge,
reduced risk of
financial irregularities
or leaks and minimize
risk of regulatory non-
compliance, etc.

9.
Macroeconomic
and geopolitical
risk
Risk
Fluctuations in
global and domestic
macroeconomic
conditions,
including
geopolitical
volatility, may
impact fuel
prices, interest
rates, inflation
and economic
stability. This can
alter purchasing
behavior, dampen
demand, increase
costs and heighten
market uncertainty,
thereby affecting
the company’s
revenue and
sustained growth.
	 Business diversification- Eternal has
strategically diversified its portfolio
across multiple verticals, catering to a
broad spectrum of customer needs, price
points and spending capacities. This
reduces reliance on any single customer
segment, helping cushion the impact of
macroeconomic fluctuations.
	 Responsive strategy and resource
allocation- Eternal actively monitors
macroeconomic trends-both short
and long-term and adjusts its business
strategy accordingly. This includes
launching or modifying product offerings
based on economic indicators and
re-allocating resources to better align
with changing consumer behavior and
preferences during volatile periods.
Negative Implication-
Ineffective
management of
macroeconomic and
geopolitical volatility
may lead to rising input
costs, compressing
margins. Heightened
uncertainty could
also delay strategic
investments and
expansion plans,
ultimately impacting
profitability, cash flows
and long-term growth
prospects.
*Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change (IPCC) RCPs (Representative Concentration Pathways) are climate scenarios used
to predict future climate conditions under different greenhouse gas emission levels. We selected a low-emissions scenario (RCP
2.6) and a high-emissions scenario (RCP 8.5) to assess the potential impact of climate change on our operations. Reference:
TCFD Report FY25 (Scenario analysis, Page 10).

Governance Leadership and Oversight
7. 	 Statement by director responsible for the business responsibility report, highlighting ESG related challenges, targets and achievements (listed entity has flexibility regarding
the placement of this disclosure)
At Eternal, we approach sustainability with the same ambition, patience and discipline we apply to building the business. We are cognizant of our responsibility in setting a
high bar in this domain for new-age, home-grown, technology companies, like ourselves. This BRSR report showcases our progress in FY26 and reflects our commitment
to grow in ways that are responsible, inclusive and sustainable.
In FY26, we conducted a double materiality assessment with an independent external organization to refresh our material ESG topics. The assessment considered the
impact of our business on people and the environment, and the implications of sustainability-related issues on long-term value creation. Our sustainability framework has,
accordingly, been refreshed and re-organized under 4 key pillars: Climate Action and Circular Economy, Inclusive Growth, Responsible Business Leadership and Corporate
Governance. I’ll be sharing ESG highlights from the food delivery business as per the refreshed framework.
Our work on Climate Action and Circular Economy focuses on minimizing our environmental impacts while preparing the business for changes in climate and resource
availability. A key climate risk mitigation action is enabling EV-based deliveries. In FY26, total EV-based food deliveries stood at 132.3 million orders, 13.5% of total FY26
food delivery orders and a 50% increase from the previous year. This achievement rode on the back of expanded EV ecosystem partnerships and the launch of our own
EV rental fleet to match the mobility needs of delivery workers, often migrants seeking to participate in the platform on a part-time and short-term basis. In addition to
our own efforts, the speed of this transition will require coordinated public and private stakeholder action to ensure the right EV products, adequate charging/swapping
infrastructure and affordable financing are made available to anyone who seeks to participate and earn from deliveries.
We are working to reduce both food and packaging waste generated by the food delivery business. Since launch in FY25, Zomato’s Food Rescue feature has helped prevent
over 7.5 lakh kg of food waste by diverting cancelled orders to interested buyers. To reduce the environmental impact of restaurant packaging, we launched Zomato’s
Plastic-Free Future Program in 2024 and have recognized more than 10,000 restaurants with a ‘low plastic packaging’ tag for switching out of plastic to pack their main
menu items . Cumulative orders from restaurants using sustainable packaging crossed 100 million in FY26.
Our platform facilitates Inclusive Growth by lowering barriers to entry, enabling access to customers and creating livelihood opportunities through food orders and
delivery. In FY26, the platform generated earnings through food delivery for 5 lakh+ restaurant partners and over 18.5 lakh unique individuals. Beyond income generation,
we supported delivery partners through insurance, government scheme access, tax filing support and savings-related products. In FY26, over 5,500 food delivery partners
were enrolled in at least one government scheme, unlocking access to benefits valued at over INR 120 crore.
Responsible Business Leadership means maintaining our business lead by fostering innovation while protecting stakeholder trust, safety and dignity. In FY26, we
launched Healthy Mode, to help customers make informed and healthier food choices, generating around 8.5 million orders. Our human rights assessment, conducted by
an independent organization, found no risks, reinforcing our strength in protecting people.  As we grew, we also enabled micro-donations to Feeding India’s network to
deliver nutritionally balanced meals to 1.4 lakh children every day. The independent initiative reached a milestone of serving 290 million meals from inception till FY26.
Corporate Governance, including topics of risk oversight, ethical conduct, cyber security and data privacy, remains central to decision-making at Eternal . In FY26 we
introduced several new measures to ensure responsible use of AI, conscious of the opportunities and risks associated with AI. Our governance and cybersecurity practices
enabled us to ensure zero data breaches in FY26.
Our efforts to date have earned us high scores in terms of ESG ratings across both Indian and global ESG rating agencies, positioning us as a leader in terms of
sustainability. We will continue to report our progress transparently, remain disciplined in our approach, and revisit our assumptions as the business and the broader
ecosystem evolve. More details on the ESG performance of Eternal’s non-food delivery businesses may be found on Pages 33 to 43 of our Annual Report.
- Sanjeev Bikhchandani, Non-Executive Director, Eternal Limited

The CSR committee, which is a sub-committee of Eternal’s Board of Directors, has oversight of ESG matters including sustainability and climate. The
CSR committee is represented by the below members:
- Sanjeev Bikhchandani, Chairman and Independent Director
- Namita Gupta, Non-Executive and Independent Director
- Aparna Popat Ved, Non-Executive and Independent Director
Mr. Deepinder Goyal stepped down as Eternal Limited’s chairman of the CSR committee on February 1, 2026, and therefore has not been included in the
FY26 list of the CSR committee.
The CSR committee meets at least once a year to review and guide Eternal’s ESG strategy and monitor plan of action and progress. At the executive
level, the Chief Sustainability Officer maintains oversight on matters related to ESG including climate, supported by the ESG team. The ESG team reports
achievements and progress to the CSR committee comprising members of Eternal’s Board of Directors.

2. 	 Does the entity have processes in place to avoid/ manage conflict of interests involving members
of the Board? (Yes/ No) If Yes, provide details of the same.
Yes. We have a policy on ‘Code of conduct for the board of directors and senior management personnel’.
Our policy covers the activities which are within the ambit of processes in place to avoid/ manage conflict
of interests involving members of the Board.
Under Section 4 ‘Code for Directors and SMP’, point number (j) mandates that Directors and SMPs must
‘Disclose potential conflicts of interest that they may have regarding any matter, if any, at the Board
Meetings and any Director having such conflict of interest will abstain himself/ herself from discussions
and voting on the concerned matter’.
All the members of the Board are required to confirm on an annual basis whether they are complying to
this code including declaration on Conflict of Interest, additionally, we ensure that all necessary approvals,
as mandated by the statute and the company’s policies are secured before engaging in transactions with
any companies in which any Board member may have a conflict.
Code of conduct for the board of directors and senior management personnel is available on our website-
link to the policy

questionnaire (aligned with the requirements of BRSR Core KPIs) enabling consistent monitoring
and improvement across our supply chain.

improvement areas.

responsible business practices.

onboarding process.

business practices and contributing to positive outcomes.

of sustainable suppliers and addressing supplier-related ESG queries.

2.	 b. 	 If yes, what percentage of inputs were sourced sustainably?
We assessed our top upstream suppliers (in terms of input) for FY25 on their sustainability credentials,
which includes SA 8000, ISO 14001/EMS, OHSAS 18001/ISO 45001 and labels like Rainforest Alliance,
Rugmark, RSPO etc. The assessment was based on voluntary disclosures by suppliers and publicly
available information. In FY26, we also introduced industry-specific questions for key suppliers to
better understand their sustainability focus areas and practices. After conducting the assessment,
we concluded that in FY25, 15.99% of our inputs were sourced sustainably.
Additionally, 65.71% of our total inputs comprised payments to delivery partners (individuals
providing food delivery services within the cities of operation), reflecting our focus on local sourcing
and supporting livelihoods in our areas of operation.

3. 	 Accessibility of workplaces
Are the premises/ offices of the entity accessible to differently-abled employees and workers, as per
the requirements of the Rights of Persons with Disabilities Act, 2016? If not, whether any steps are
being taken by the entity in this regard.
Our headquarters and largest office based in Gurugram underwent a third party accessibility audit in
FY24. We have implemented various measures such as ramps/ tactile pathways, disability-friendly
elevators, gender-neutral accessible toilets and accessible parking at our Gurugram office. Additionally,
all new facilities under our direct lease have been evaluated for accessibility standards outlined in the
RPwD Act, 2016.

Yes. Eternal has an Equal opportunity, diversity and inclusion policy aligned with the principles of Rights
of Persons with Disabilities Act, 2016. (link to the policy)

Company Overview
10. 	Health and safety management system:
a. 	 Whether an occupational health and safety management system has been implemented by
the entity? (Yes/ No). If yes, the coverage of such a system?
Yes. At Eternal, our Health and Safety Policy includes nine pillars that outline how we effectively manage
critical Occupational Health and Safety (OH&S) aspects. Additionally, we hold the ISO 45001:2018
certification for our OH&S Management System. The Audit Committee of Eternal Limited maintains
oversight on health and safety related matters and reviews them on a quarterly basis.

The Environment, Health and Safety (EHS) team helps the organization maintain high levels of safety
standards. Our offices are equipped with essential safety systems such as fire detection, fire fighting
mechanisms, public address systems, safety signage, etc., to ensure the safety of employees and
workers. Additionally, there is a Safety Reporting System (SRS) that enables employees and workers
to report OH&S incidents and hazards actively. The system ensures seamless tracking, analysis and
resolution, thereby building a proactive safety culture and enabling continuous improvement of our
OH&S management system.
Health and Safety training is mandatory for all employees of Eternal and a key pillar of our Health &
Safety policy. In FY26, we strengthened our safety culture through a series of focused awareness
initiatives. We marked National Road Safety month, with a mix of on-ground and digital engagement
activities, including interactive carnivals, recognition of road safety champions under the company-
wide “Sadak Suraksha Jeevan Raksha” campaign. During this month, we also conducted engaging
awareness initiatives across offices, emphasizing people safety and SRS reporting, while reinforcing
the message that “safety is a habit we build together.” These efforts are further supported by
continuous monitoring by the Workplace & Facilities team to ensure sustained adherence to safety
standards.
b. 	 What are the processes used to identify work-related hazards and assess risks on a routine and
non-routine basis by the entity?
Risk identification and mitigation remain integral to our Health and Safety policy.  We adopt a proactive
approach to identifying, assessing and mitigating hazards across both routine and non-routine
activities. An integrated action plan is also in place to effectively respond to emergency situations.
As part of our ISO 45001:2018-certified Occupational Health and Safety Management System, we
follow documented procedures to systematically evaluate work-related hazards and risks across all
operations. Employees and workers are encouraged to report hazards, near-misses and incidents
through our Safety Reporting System (SRS), enabling structured investigation and timely closure of
such cases.
We continuously strengthen our approach through regular internal inspections and audits to
proactively identify and address workplace risks. Identified risks are managed through structured
action plans with defined timelines and measurable targets to ensure effective mitigation.
c. 	 Whether you have processes for workers to report the work-related hazards and to remove
themselves from such risks. (Y/ N)
Yes, we have established a robust process for workers to report work-related hazards and protect
themselves from associated risks. In FY24, we introduced the Safety Reporting System (SRS), enabling
employees and workers to report health and safety hazards, incidents, and near-misses.
Building on this, the process is now well established across the organization. During FY26, we further
strengthened its implementation through multiple awareness sessions, townhalls and training
programs, ensuring it remains a reliable and accessible platform. We also ensured timely closure of
identified risks, supported by effective Root Cause Analysis (RCA) and implementation of Corrective
and Preventive Actions (CAPA).


119
Financial Statements
Statutory Reports: BRSR
Company Overview
As part of our annual safety campaigns, we launched the “7 Habits of Eternal Safety Champions”
in FY25, reinforcing that safety is a shared responsibility built through consistent behaviors and
collective commitment. This is a successful ongoing campaign in FY26. This initiative has significantly
strengthened safety awareness, encouraged proactive hazard identification and inculcated a culture
of shared responsibility across the organization.
d. 	 Do the employees/ workers of the entity have access to non-occupational medical and healthcare
services?  (Yes/ No)
Yes. Eternal provides its employees with non-occupational medical and healthcare benefits. These
include comprehensive group medical insurance that extends to the employee, spouse/ partner and
up to four children. The medical insurance has progressive coverages such as treatment for autism
and psychiatric ailments, lifelong coverage for children living with disabilities, etc. We also offer free
online medical consultations, nutrition counselling, psychological counselling, discounted diagnostic
tests and discounts on medicines, etc.
Over and above, all employees are covered under the group accidental insurance. We also provide
medical insurance or ESI benefits to our other than permanent workers basis applicability.

12. 	Describe the measures taken by the entity to ensure a safe and healthy workplace.
At Eternal, we prioritize the safety and welfare of our employees and their overall well-being. We have
implemented comprehensive policies and protocols aimed at fostering an incident-free, vibrant and
secure workplace environment. We have dedicated teams which are responsible for monitoring safety
performance and implementing necessary measures to enhance safety protocols. The following outline
several initiatives designed to promote a culture of health, safety and wellbeing of employees within our
organization:

mock drills to assess our readiness and improve our response capabilities

15. 	Provide details of any corrective action taken or underway to address safety-related incidents
(if any) and on significant risks/ concerns arising from assessments of health & safety practices
and working conditions.
We have conducted comprehensive risk assessments and revised risk evaluations across all activities in
our offices to ensure continued compliance with safety standards and best practices. Through our internal
assessment we have confirmed that our operations align with the established safety protocols since there
were zero safety-related incidents reported in FY26 (Principle 3, Q11), therefore no immediate corrective
actions or mitigation measures were required.
We hold ISO 45001:2018 certification for our Gurugram and Bengaluru offices. For our newly leased
offices we are in the process of attaining these certifications. Our Occupational Health and Safety (OH&S)
management system remains aligned with the requirements of the standard, reaffirming the robustness
of the framework.
In addition, an independent Human Rights assessment has been conducted for all our office locations and
no major findings were reported during the financial year.

The company also provides a generous ex gratia payment to the families of all our deceased employees.

consultations provided to the Board.
We regularly engage with our key stakeholders (end-users, restaurant partners and delivery partners)
as outlined in our Stakeholder management policy. Each business unit in every operating location is
required to carry out stakeholder engagement in line with the stakeholder management policy. Through
the channels described in the policy, the functional teams in Eternal receive feedback from various groups
of stakeholders (external and internal) on our initiatives and operations. This feedback is analyzed and
discussed with relevant leadership teams to identify, develop and implement the environmental and
social topics. Select actions are discussed with the CSR committee of the board based on their overall
importance and these meetings are minuted. The CSR Committee at Eternal also undertakes responsibility
for climate and sustainability-related matters.

Material topic: Climate action and circular economy
	 Zomato Plastic-Free Future Program: In response
to customer concerns around receiving food from
restaurants in plastic packaging, we launched the
‘Zomato Plastic-Free Future Program’ in December
2024. The program recognizes restaurant partners
actively adopting sustainable alternatives to
plastic packaging for food deliveries. More than
350 restaurant brands, representing 10,000+
restaurants across 500+ cities listed on the Zomato
app have already joined the program by submitting
detailed evidence of their packaging, including
photos and certificates, confirming that their
main menu items are being packed in sustainable
alternatives. To recognize their efforts, these
restaurants now carry a prominent ‘Low plastic
packaging’ tag on their menu pages, banners and a
clear label in the order summary, making it easy for
customers to identify these restaurants. Customers
can also discover these restaurants faster by typing
‘Low plastic packaging’ in the search bar or using the
‘Low plastic packaging’ filter option. As of March 31,
2026, we have facilitated 100 million food delivery
orders through restaurants on the Zomato Plastic-
Free Future Program.
Material topic: Innovation
	 Healthy mode: To capture the growing demand for
healthier meals, Eternal launched “Healthy mode”
on the Zomato app in top 7 cities in FY26. With this
mode, end users see a curation of  restaurants along
with complete nutritional information, i.e. macros,
ingredient-level description and customization
filters. Since its launch, Healthy Mode has helped
customers make more informed and healthier food
choices, generating ~8.5 million orders in FY26.
Delivery partners

	 Surveys (including

	 Focus Group

Material topic: Stakeholder value creation
	 Social & Financial security through Government
scheme facilitation: In FY26, in response to
requests for support from our delivery partners,
Eternal designed and launched a structured
program to facilitate their better access to central
and state government schemes. This initiative was
designed to address key barriers faced by partners
in accessing schemes including lack of awareness,
time scarcity, documentation challenges and
unclear requirements.
The program, run in collaboration with the social
enterprise, Haqdarshak ran through a series of
on-ground facilitation camps and digital support
mechanisms across India. In FY26, over 5,500
Zomato delivery partners were enrolled in at least
one government scheme, unlocking benefits valued
at over INR 120 crore. These benefits span critical
areas such as healthcare, insurance and income
support.

Material topic: Stakeholder value creation
	 Improvements in app accessibility for Persons
with Disabilities (PwD) - Based on feedback from
the community of Persons with Disabilities, in
FY26 we launched a feature on the Zomato food
delivery app, that enables customers to disclose
their mobility, vision and hearing needs to receive
a customized platform and delivery experience.
Additionally, select members of Eternal’s tech,
product and design teams underwent the
IAAP CPACC certification in accessibility. The
certification verifies a professional’s broad, cross-
disciplinary knowledge of disabilities, universal
design and global accessibility laws. Completion of
this training empowers team members to practically
apply accessibility principles in the course of
product development.
3. 	 Provide details of instances of engagement with and actions are taken to, address the concerns
of vulnerable/ marginalized stakeholder groups.
Eternal has identified vulnerable/ marginalized stakeholders groups both within and outside of its value
chain and has taken multiple actions to address their concerns.
Below are the actions taken to address concerns of vulnerable/ marginalized groups within Eternal’s value
chain:

Zomato’s Equitable Action for Livelihood (ZEAL) program- Launched in FY23, our PwD delivery
partner initiative has benefitted over 6,000 PwDs with a range of disabilities including locomotor
and hearing and speech impairment. In FY26 alone 3,500+ PwD DPs, collectively delivered over 10
million orders and covered over 40 million kms. The program benefits include waiver of onboarding
fee, assignment of short-distance deliveries only, enhanced payouts, sensitized fleet coaches and
support teams to better assist delivery partners as needed. In addition, restaurant partners and end-
users are notified when a PwD delivery partner is assigned to an order and requested to extend their
support.

Zomato’s Women riding centers- Facilitates two-wheeler riding training for women, basic digital
literacy and confidence building. Through this initiative, Eternal aims to empower women by enhancing
their mobility, enabling them to meet their personal needs with greater independence while also
unlocking access to livelihood opportunities. Highlighted below are the benefits offered at these
centers:

A dedicated space for women and their families to meet with company representatives, inquire
and seek guidance on working in the gig economy.

A safe space for women to connect, support each other, and build a sense of community.

Guidance to test and understand the available EV-bike options for interested participants.
Other initiatives to ensure women safety and support:

24×7 SOS emergency support: Available in 900+ cities, the emergency response system connects
delivery partners to support like ambulance, police and an in-house response team.

Address visibility at night: Between 7 PM and 5 AM, women delivery partners have the option to avoid
locations where they may feel unsafe.


128
Financial Statements
Statutory Reports: BRSR
Company Overview

City-specific chat support groups: All women delivery partners in the top seven cities are added to
WhatsApp support groups for seamless communication, grievance redressal and peer support.

Hotel delivery protocol: Women delivery partners delivering to hotels, lodges or guest houses can
complete deliveries at the reception instead of going to rooms.

Black zones reporting: Delivery partners can report unsafe or difficult-to-navigate areas through
the delivery partner app. More than 270 black zones were identified as of 31st March 2026, with such
locations marked unserviceable, where required, to enhance safety.
Actions taken to address the concerns of vulnerable/marginalized groups outside of the value chain:

Eternal apps allow customers to contribute to Feeding India, a not-for-profit initiative, directly at the
checkout page. These contributions support Feeding India’s mission, focused on eradicating hunger
and malnutrition among children from under-served communities across the country. Through Feeding
India, 1.4 lakh+ children now receive nourishing meals daily across 2,300+ centers in 150+ cities. In
FY26, we deepened this work by revamping 45 Anganwadis across Varanasi, Nandurbar, Kushinagar
and Gurugram. Since its inception, Feeding India has served over 290 million meals till FY26. We also
deployed facial recognition technology across educational centers to track meal distribution digitally,
ensuring both accountability and scale without adding administrative burden.
For further details of key programs and initiatives, please refer to the Feeding India Annual Report FY25

5. 	 Describe the internal mechanisms in place to redress grievances related to human rights
issues.
The following are the internal mechanisms in place at Eternal to redress grievances related to human
rights issues:

We have a dedicated HR business partnering team to address and investigate any human rights
complaints.

POSH related concerns can be reported at speakup@eternal.com, while human rights related issues
can be reported at hr@eternal.com.

All emails received in these inboxes are systematically categorized and routed to the relevant
stakeholder for redressal.

The team is responsible for conducting a thorough investigation, determining appropriate resolutions,
and implementing necessary corrective actions.

All complaints are kept confidential and investigated promptly.

In addition, we have a grievance mechanism in place for our value chain partners to report instances
related to human rights.
These internal mechanisms are detailed in our Human rights policy. (link to the policy)

8. 	 Mechanisms to prevent adverse consequences to the complainant in discrimination and
harassment cases.
Our commitment to prevent discrimination and harassment at workplace is demonstrated in our documented
policies on Prevention of Sexual Harassment (POSH), Human Rights and Equal Opportunity, Diversity and
Inclusion. We have strong anti-retaliation measures built into our policies. We are committed to protecting
the complainant and ensuring no retaliation of any kind against anyone reporting good faith concerns.
Anyone targeting such a person will be subjected to strict disciplinary actions. This protection is extended
not only to the complainant but also to anyone who assists with or cooperates as part of an investigation.

11. 	 Provide details of any corrective actions taken or underway to address significant risks/
concerns arising from the assessments at Question 10 above.
As per the results of the Human Rights Assessment conducted by an independent external organization
in FY26, there were no significant/ high risks identified at Eternal Limited (formerly known as Zomato
Limited). As an organization we are committed to promoting and respecting human rights of all employees
and workers. We conduct regular training and awareness sessions pertaining to various human rights
aspects in order to sensitize our workforce.
Leadership Indicators
1. 	 Details of a business process being modified/ introduced as a result of addressing human rights
grievances/ complaints.
We continuously monitor, review and update our policies and processes to ensure a workplace free from
human rights concerns. Our existing frameworks are robust, requiring no major changes to business
processes while effectively addressing and resolving potential issues.
To reinforce our commitment, we have procedures in place, supported by grievance redressal mechanisms,
training and awareness programs for employees. Our initiatives ensure that all stakeholders are informed of
their rights and responsibilities, fostering a culture of respect, compliance, and ethical business conduct.
2. 	 Details of the scope and coverage of any Human rights due-diligence conducted.
In FY26, our human rights due diligence exercise was conducted by an independent third party, and no
significant risks were identified. Due-diligence for Eternal Limited (formerly known as Zomato Limited)
covered all our permanent employees, other than permanent employees and third party workers (including
migrant workers) of all genders, across all locations.
Following aspects were covered as part of the due-diligence conducted:

Working conditions: Working hours, work-related stress, degree of safety, and comfort

Labor rights: Employment contracts, freedom of association and collective bargaining, employee
benefits, child labor, human trafficking - forced or bonded labor

Rights to privacy: Protection of personal and professional data and information

Health, safety and well-being: Training and awareness sessions, safety drills, insurances, assistance
programmes and policies.

Fair and equal remuneration: Compensation practices

Freedom of speech: Grievance and feedback mechanisms

Workplace discrimination and harassment: Discrimination (age, gender, ethnicity, sexual preference,
color, disability, etc.) as well as mental and sexual harassment

Learning and development: Skill development programs for career progression and professional growth

Diversity and inclusion: Equal opportunity, diversity and inclusion
In addition to an annual Human rights due diligence exercise, we also periodically review our policies and
procedures to ensure our risk mapping of potential issues (if any) are updated and mitigated accordingly.
3. 	 Is the premise/ office of the entity accessible to differently abled visitors, as per the requirements
of the Rights of Persons with Disabilities Act, 2016?
Our headquarters and largest office based in Gurugram, underwent an independent external accessibility
audit in FY24. We have implemented various measures such as ramps/ tactile pathways, disability-friendly
elevators, gender-neutral accessible toilets and accessible parking at our Gurugram office. Additionally,
all new facilities under our direct lease have been evaluated for accessibility standards outlined in the
RPwD Act, 2016.

8. 	 Does the entity have any project related to reducing greenhouse gas emission? If Yes, then
provide details.
Yes, below are the details of our projects-

Project 1: Carbon market instruments
We have maintained 100% of our Scope 1 & Scope 2 emissions at zero through use of an equivalent
amount of verified carbon removal offsets and International Renewable Energy Certificates (IRECs).

Project 2: Transition to EV based food deliveries
Our key climate risk mitigation action is to support delivery partners in transitioning to EV bikes to
undertake food deliveries. To achieve this, we generate awareness about EVs among delivery partners,
collaborate with EV ecosystem players to secure access to EVs on rent and advocate for better EV
products, infrastructure and financing.

EV awareness: In FY26, delivery partners were made aware of the benefits of transitioning to
EVs through multiple engaging videos on the YouTube channel, EV campaigns launched within the
Zomato delivery partner app and also through notifications. We also organized the second edition
of EV Bazaar in Bengaluru to educate the delivery partners on the benefits of EVs and to support
their transition. Since its inception, the EV Bazaar initiative has achieved significant outreach,
engaging over 2,000 delivery partners and more than 20 ecosystem providers across Delhi and
Bengaluru.

EV access: To facilitate easier access to EVs on rent, we have established partnerships with
EV rental agencies, battery swapping companies and EV-based logistics service providers. We
launched the Zomato EV rental fleet on World Environment Day 2025 in Delhi to enable rental
access to quality high-speed EVs at affordable prices for delivery partners. As of March 2026,
more than 1,500 EVs have been deployed across Delhi and Chennai under the Zomato EV rental
fleet initiative.

EV advocacy: We continuously advocate for better EV products, infrastructure and financing for
delivery partners. We are a part of global coalitions on EVs including the Climate Group’s EV100
initiative and the UNEP-supported Deliver-e Coalition. We regularly contribute to stakeholder
consultations facilitated by central, state and city-level government on the topic of EVs.

Delivery optimization: Alongside supporting partners in adopting EVs for food delivery, we are
continuously improving operational efficiency through delivery optimization measures that bring
down delivery distances and time, thereby reducing energy consumption and its associated
emissions. In FY26, delivery optimization has resulted in avoidance of 8,100 tCO2e of emissions.
As a result of our efforts, our last-mile delivery emissions on a per km basis reduced by 11.77% in
FY26 when compared to FY22. The total EV-based food deliveries increased by 50% compared to the
previous year, totaling 132.3 million orders. This represented 13.5% of total food delivery orders for
the same year.

10.	 Briefly describe the waste management practices adopted in your establishments. Describe
the strategy adopted by your company to reduce the usage of hazardous and toxic chemicals
in your products and processes and the practices adopted to manage such wastes.
Eternal Limited (formerly known as Zomato Limited) is a technology platform services provider. Our core
business  processes do not result in the generation of waste beyond office waste within which no toxic
waste is generated and hazardous waste is minimal.
However, we are deeply committed to effective waste management strategies to minimize environmental
impacts of waste in our offices and in our value chain.
Office waste management
Our employees sit out of leased premises or co-working spaces where waste disposal is managed by the
owners or main lessors of the property. In offices that are directly leased by us, we have implemented
initiatives to reduce waste and promote segregation at the source.

We promote the use of sustainable alternatives within our workplaces through provision of reusable
cutlery and plates, implementation of a ‘Bring Your Own Bottle (BYOB)’ campaign, use of printing paper
made from wheat straw (agri-residue). Our office coffee mugs are biodegradable and made of fallen
pine needles, thereby reducing reliance on single-use items and virgin resources.

We utilize color coded and labelled bins to ensure segregation of waste streams, supported by
dedicated housekeeping teams. For e-waste, biomedical and hazardous waste, we appoint authorized
vendors and obtain disposal and recycling certificates. For other waste streams such as plastic, wet
waste and paper and cardboard, that are managed by landlords or facility operators, we obtain details
on disposal and recycling from their appointed vendors.

We conduct regular awareness campaigns and training initiatives on responsible consumption
and disposal habits among our employees. These efforts are aimed at promoting efficient waste
segregation at source.

We continue to invest in innovative and sustainable waste management practices. For example,
in FY26, we onboarded Padcare as our menstrual waste management vendor across our offices.
Padcare collects used sanitary pads and recycles them into hygienic pulp and plastic, offering a safer
alternative to landfilling and incineration.
In line with our environmental best practices, our Gurugram and Bengaluru offices hold ISO 14001
certification, demonstrating our commitment to effective environmental management.


140
Financial Statements
Statutory Reports: BRSR
Company Overview
Value chain waste management
Packaging and food waste are the two kinds of waste associated with food delivery orders sent by
restaurants to end-consumers via the Zomato platform. Restaurants choose the kind of packaging they
wish to use to deliver food. Restaurant practices also determine the amount of waste generated in the
cooking process. End-consumers control the number of orders they may place, cancellation decisions and
process of disposal of packaging and leftovers (after consuming food). We run several voluntary initiatives
to positively influence behaviours of both stakeholders with respect to waste management.

Reducing food and cutlery waste

The Zomato app offers consumers the option of making special requests at the time of ordering,
including a request to the restaurant not to send cutlery, thereby reducing cutlery waste. Between
FY22 and FY26, this initiative has helped avoid over 3,000 MT of cutlery waste.

Additionally, The ‘Food Rescue’ initiative further addresses food wastage by making canceled
orders available for nearby customers in their original untampered packaging at discounted
prices. Since launch in FY25, Zomato’s Food Rescue feature has helped prevent over 7.5 lakh kg
of food waste by diverting cancelled orders to interested buyers (assuming each order weighs
approximately 400g).

Rewarding adopters and innovators of sustainable packaging

We introduced the Zomato Plastic-Free Future Program in December 2024 for restaurant partners
actively adopting sustainable alternatives to plastic packaging for food deliveries. To recognize
their efforts, these restaurants now carry a prominent ‘Low plastic packaging’ tag on their menu
pages, banners and a clear label in the order summary, making it easy for customers to identify
these restaurants. Customers can also discover these restaurants faster by typing ‘Low plastic
packaging’ in the search bar or using the ‘Low plastic packaging’ filter option. As of March 31st
2026, over 100 million ‘Low plastic packaging’ orders had been delivered from restaurants that
had signed up to the Plastic-Free Future Program.

To promote innovation on alternatives to plastic packaging for the food delivery use-case, we
run an annual event on sustainable packaging bringing start-up innovators and restaurateurs to
the same platform. In FY26, Zomato Plastic-Free Future Packathon was held in partnership with
Startup India and Hyperpure by Zomato attracting applications from more than 125 sustainable
packaging start-ups all over India.

Voluntary recycling

Plastic continues to play an important role in packaging for food deliveries for a few important
reasons. Many restaurants, especially those that offer Indian and Oriental cuisine, choose plastic
containers, trays and bottles for food deliveries on account of lack of suitable sustainable
alternatives for liquids and ‘wet’ cuisine such as  gravies, soups and beverages. Apart from
functional reasons, plastic is relatively affordable and more available across India. In order to
offset the impact of plastic packaging used by restaurants on the environment, in FY23, we
launched a voluntary plastic waste recycling initiative called the 100% plastic neutral deliveries
initiative. Since launch, Eternal has voluntarily recycled 60,000 MT of plastic waste from FY23-
FY26 (15,000 MT was recycled in FY26 alone) fully meeting the target of 100% plastic neutral
deliveries as per independent estimates.

undertaken
Details of the initiative (Web-link, if any, may be
provided along-with summary)
Outcome of the initiative
Rewarding
adopters and
innovators of
sustainable
packaging

Plastic Free Future Packathon: While
restaurants choose their own packaging,
alternatives to plastic packaging are limited
in the market. To promote innovation
on alternatives to plastic packaging for
the food delivery use-case, we run the
Zomato Plastic-Free Future Packathon
in partnership with Startup India and
Hyperpure by Zomato which serves over
2.6 lakh+ restaurants. This annual contest
surfaces and spotlights startups working on
innovative sustainable packaging solutions
for food delivery.

Plastic Free Future Packathon: The
second edition of the Packathon
recognized two winners across two
categories - ready-to-scale and prototype
- from 128 applicants from 21 states. 47
start-ups were led by women.
Ready-to-scale startup winners Imaga
and Go Do Good showcased bamboo
fiber-based food delivery packaging that
can effectively pack oily food items, and
plant-based barrier coatings that are oil
and grease resistant respectively. In the
prototype category, Agrileaf Exports
presented takeaway containers made
of areca palm leaves with heat-sealable
lids, and Earthworks Biomat showcased
deliveryware made from natural fibers and
biopolymers derived from agri-waste.
Additionally, we presented the first Zomato
Plastic-Free Packaging Awards to 24
national and regional restaurant brands that
have successfully shifted to sustainable
packaging alternatives while ensuring a high-
quality customer experience.
Across the two editions of the Zomato
Packathon, many exciting innovative
solutions have been highlighted, including
edible cutlery, barrier coatings made of
plant-based gums and seaweed and patented
carry bags made of agri-waste. These
innovations were displayed to all attendees
through an extensive exhibition featuring
41 enterprises working on plastic-free
packaging solutions for food delivery. In
FY26, Zomato Plastic-Free Future Packathon
was held in partnership with Startup India
and Hyperpure by Zomato attracting
applications from more than 125 sustainable
packaging start-ups all over India.
The Zomato Plastic-Free Future Program
(and the Zomato Packathon) is a one-of-a-
kind initiative by a food delivery platform
globally to actively encourage the restaurant
industry to adopt sustainable alternatives
while working on unlocking progress for the
Indian sustainable packaging ecosystem.
5.
Reducing food
and cutlery
waste

Reducing cutlery waste: The Zomato app
offers consumers the option of making
special requests at the time of ordering,
including a request to the restaurant not to
send cutlery, thereby reducing cutlery waste.

Food rescue initiative: Food wastage is a
significant challenge, given that every month,
around 4,00,000 perfectly good orders already
on their way to customers are canceled on the
Zomato app. To reduce this food wastage, we
introduced ‘Food Rescue’, an initiative that
makes canceled orders available for nearby
customers in their original untampered
packaging at unbeatable prices within minutes.

Reducing cutlery waste: Between FY22
and FY26, this initiative has helped avoid
over 3,000 MT of cutlery waste in total.

Food rescue initiative: Since launch in
FY25, Zomato’s Food Rescue feature has
helped prevent over 7.5 lakh kg of food
waste by diverting cancelled orders to
interested buyers (assuming each order
weighs approximately 400g).

5.	 Does the entity have a business continuity and disaster management plan? Give details in 100
words/ web link.
Yes. Eternal Limited has established a Business Continuity Management (BCM) Program, which includes
formal Business Continuity and Crisis Management plans. The framework is designed to proactively
identify threats and mitigate risks associated with potential business disruptions (including climate). Our
program includes regular Risk Assessments and Business Impact Analysis (BIA) to evaluate and manage
financial, operational and reputational consequences. The plans define clear recovery strategies to ensure
the swift restoration of critical business operations (including technology systems). Furthermore, we
maintain organizational readiness through periodic testing, training and oversight by a dedicated team.

entity. What mitigation or adaptation measures have been taken by the entity in this regard.
Eternal’s value chain does not have any adverse impact on the environment. We proactively help our value
chain partners in reducing their environmental impact through collaborative efforts. These initiatives have
been described in the above table (Principle 6, Leadership indicator 4).

support received. Trends derived from the analysis of the feedback and ratings are used to implement
corrective measures as required.

grievances autonomously. This has resulted in faster query resolution, personalized support, improved
customer satisfaction and delivered significant cost savings. Nugget is also available to businesses
outside of Eternal, enabling them to enhance their customer support operations effortlessly.

INDEPENDENT PRACTITIONER’S ASSURANCE REPORT ON IDENTIFIED
SUSTAINABILITY INFORMATION IN ETERNAL LIMITED’S BUSINESS RESPONSIBILITY
AND SUSTAINABILITY REPORT
To the Board of Directors
of ETERNAL LIMITED (formerly known as Zomato
Limited)
1.	 We have undertaken to perform assurance
engagement, for ETERNAL LIMITED (the
“Company”) vide our engagement letter dated
February 13, 2026, in respect of the agreed
Sustainability Information listed below (the
“Identified Sustainability Information”) in
accordance with the criteria stated in paragraph 3
below. This Sustainability Information is included
in the Business Responsibility & Sustainability
Report (or the “BRSR Report”) within the Annual
Report of the Company for the year ended March
31, 2026. This engagement was conducted by
our multidisciplinary team including assurance
practitioners, environmental engineers and
specialists.
2.	 Identified Sustainability Information
Our scope of reasonable and limited assurance
consists of the Sustainability Information listed
in Appendix I and Appendix II respectively in our
report. The reporting boundary of the Report is
as disclosed in Question 13 of Section A: General
Disclosure of the BRSR Report with exceptions
disclosed by way of note under respective
questions of the BRSR, where applicable.
Our assurance engagement was with respect to
the year ended March 31, 2026, information only
as listed in Appendix I unless otherwise stated
and we have not performed any procedures with
respect to any other elements included in the
BRSR Report and, therefore, do not express any
opinion/ conclusion thereon.
3.	 Criteria
The Criteria used by the Company to prepare the
Identified Sustainability Information is as under:

Regulation 34(2)(f) of the Securities and
Exchange Board of India (the "SEBI") (Listing
Obligations and Disclosure Requirements),
Regulations, 2015 as amended;

Business Responsibility and Sustainability
Reporting Requirements for listed entities
per Master Circular No. HO/49/14/14(7)2025-
CFD-POD2/I/3762/2026 dated January 30,
2026 (the “SEBI Master Circular”); and

GHG Protocol Corporate Accounting and
Reporting Standard as amended thereto,
and its supplement, the Corporate Value
Chain (Scope 3) Accounting and Reporting
Standard, both developed by GHG Protocol
Initiative
4.	 Management’s Responsibility
The Company’s management is responsible
for selecting or establishing suitable criteria
for preparing the Sustainability Information
including the reporting boundary of the BRSR
Report, taking into account applicable laws and
regulations, if any, related to reporting on the
Sustainability Information, identification of key
aspects, engagement with stakeholders, content,
preparation and presentation of the Identified
Sustainability Information in accordance with
the Criteria. This responsibility includes design,
implementation and maintenance of internal
controls relevant to the preparation of the BRSR
Report and the measurement of Identified
Sustainability Information, which is free from
material misstatement, whether due to fraud or
error.
5.	 Inherent limitations
The absence of a significant body of established
practice on which to draw to evaluate and measure
non-financial information allows for different,
but acceptable, measures and measurement
techniques and can affect comparability between
companies.
6.	 Our Independence and Quality Control
We have maintained our independence and
confirm that we have met the requirements of the
Code of Ethics issued by the Institute of Chartered
Accountants of India (the “ICAI”) and the SEBI
Master Circular and its clarifications thereto and


154
Financial Statements
Statutory Reports: BRSR
Company Overview
have the required competencies and experience
to conduct this assurance engagement.
We apply Standard on Quality Control (“SQC”) 1,
“Quality Control for Firms that Perform Audits
and Reviews of Historical Financial Information,
and Other Assurance and Related Services
Engagements”, and accordingly maintain
a comprehensive system of quality control
including documented policies and procedures
regarding compliance with ethical requirements,
professional standards, and applicable legal and
regulatory requirements.
7.	 Our Responsibility
Our responsibility is to express a reasonable
assurance opinion and limited assurance
conclusion on the Identified Sustainability
Information listed in Appendix I and Appendix II
respectively based on the procedures we have
performed and evidence we have obtained.
We conducted our engagement in accordance
with the Standard on Sustainability Assurance
Engagements (SSAE) 3000, “Assurance
Engagements on Sustainability Information”,
and Standard on Assurance Engagements (SAE)
3410 Assurance Engagements on Greenhouse
Gas Statements (together the “Standards”), both
issued by the Sustainability Reporting Standards
Board (the “SRSB”) of the ICAI.
These Standards require that we plan and perform
our engagement to obtain reasonable assurance
about whether the Identified Sustainability
Information listed in Appendix I and included in the
BRSR Report are prepared, in all material respects,
in accordance with the Criteria; and obtain
limited assurance about whether the Identified
Sustainability Information listed in Appendix II and
included in the BRSR Report is free from material
misstatement.
As part of both reasonable and limited assurance
engagement in accordance with the Standards,
we exercise professional judgment and maintain
professional skepticism throughout the
engagement.
Reasonable Assurance
A reasonable assurance engagement involves
identifying and assessing the risks of material
misstatement of the Identified Sustainability
Information whether due to fraud or error,
responding to the assessed risks as necessary
in the circumstances.
The procedures we performed were based on our
professional judgment and included inquiries,
observation of processes performed, inspection
of documents, evaluating the appropriateness
of quantification methods and reporting policies
and agreeing or reconciling with underlying
records.
Given the circumstances of the engagement, in
performing the procedures listed above, we:

Obtained an understanding of the Identified
Sustainability Information and related
disclosures;

Obtained an understanding of the assessment
criteria and their suitability for the evaluation
and/or measurements of the Identified
Sustainability Information;

Made inquiries of Company’s management,
including the corporate sustainability team,
Environment, Health and Safety (EHS) team,
the compliance team, the human resource
team, the finance team amongst others and
those with the responsibility for preparation
of the BRSR Report;

Obtained an understanding and performed an
evaluation of the design of the key systems,
processes and controls for recording,
processing and reporting on the Identified
Sustainability Information at the corporate
office and other offices on a sample basis.
This included evaluating the design of
those controls relevant to the engagement
and determining whether they have been
implemented by performing procedures
in addition to inquiry of the personnel
responsible for the Identified Sustainability
Information;

the risks that the Identified Sustainability

on sample basis under the reporting boundary
for ensuring the completeness and accuracy
of data being reported.
We believe that the evidence we have obtained is
sufficient and appropriate to provide a basis for
our reasonable assurance opinion.
Limited Assurance
A limited assurance engagement involves
assessing the suitability in the circumstances of
the Company’s use of the Criteria as the basis for
the preparation of the Identified Sustainability
Information as listed in Appendix II, assessing the
risks of material misstatement of the Identified
Sustainability Information whether due to fraud
or error, responding to the assessed risks as
necessary in the circumstances, and evaluating
the overall presentation of the Identified
Sustainability Information.
A limited assurance engagement is substantially
less in scope than a reasonable assurance
engagement in relation to both the risk assessment
procedures, including an understanding of
internal controls, and the procedures performed
in response to the assessed risks.
The procedures we performed were based on our
professional judgment and included inquiries,
observation of processes performed, inspection
of documents and evaluating the appropriateness
of quantification methods and reporting policies
and agreeing with underlying records.
Given the circumstances of the engagement, in
performing the procedures listed above, we:

Obtained an understanding of the Identified
Sustainability Information and related
disclosures;

Obtained an understanding of the assessment
criteria and their suitability for the evaluation
and/or measurements of the Identified
Sustainability Information;

Made inquiries of Company’s management,
including the Corporate Sustainability team,
Environment, Health & Safety (EHS) team,
the compliance team, the human resource
team, the finance team amongst others and
those with the responsibility for preparation
of the BRSR Report;

Obtained an understanding of the key
systems, processes and controls for
recording, processing and reporting on the
Identified Sustainability Information at the
corporate office and other offices of the
Company on a sample basis. Our procedures
did not include evaluating the suitability
of design, obtaining evidence about their
implementation or testing operating
effectiveness of particular control activities;

Based on the above understanding and
the risks that the Identified Sustainability
Information may be materially misstated,
determined the nature, timing, and extent of
further procedures;

Reviewed the key assumptions, emission
factors and methodologies used for
calculation of GHG emissions; and

Reviewed the Company’s process for
collating the sustainability information
through agreeing or reconciling the
Identified Sustainability Information with the
underlying records on a sample basis.
The procedures performed in a limited assurance
engagement vary in nature and timing from, and
are less in extent than for, a reasonable assurance
engagement. Consequently, the level of assurance
obtained in a limited assurance engagement
is substantially lower than the assurance that
would have been obtained had we performed a

to whom our report is shown or into whose hands

Report on Corporate Governance (CGR)
1.	Company’s
philosophy
on
Corporate Governance
Our corporate governance philosophy is rooted
in integrity, transparency, accountability, and
responsible business conduct, ensuring the protection
of stakeholder interests and driving long-term value
creation. This commitment to ethical and sustainable
practices shapes our organizational decision-making
and drives compliance with applicable laws in line with
the charter documents of the Company in both letter
and spirit. At the core of our governance framework
is the strategic oversight and independent judgement
provided by our Board, who guide the Company’s
affairs and supervise the leadership team’s day-to-
day operations through specialized committees.
To further foster a robust culture of accountability,
we have implemented a comprehensive policy
framework that includes specific codes of conduct
for employees, senior management, and the Board,
alongside strict guidelines for business partners and
for prevention of insider trading.
2.	Board of Directors
The Board steers the strategy, performance, and
governance, establishing a robust operational
framework to drive sustainable, long-term value for
all stakeholders. The Board maintains independent
oversight to ensure management consistently
delivers on its commitments. The directors bring
together a diverse mix of specialized skills and deep
industry experience with strong leadership to execute
its responsibilities with high efficiency.
The Board has 7 (Seven) committees comprising of
Board members to discharge the responsibilities
assigned to them.
Composition and inter-se relationship
During the financial year under review, the composition
of the Board was in conformity with Regulation 17 of
the SEBI Listing Regulations and Section 149 and 152
of the Act. The Company operates with a one-tier
system featuring an optimum mix of non-executive
directors including women directors.
The Board comprises 6 (six) directors of whom 4 (four)
are independent directors (including 3 (three) women
independent directors) and 2 (two) non-executive
directors, (including 1 (one) nominee director). None
of the directors share any inter-se relationships, each
one of them is independent of the other, and all non-
executive directors are under the age of seventy-five
years.
The detailed profiles of our directors and individual
committee compositions can be accessed at Link.
Board Meetings
Our Board meets at regular intervals to deliberate
and decide on operations, key strategic issues, and
statutory matters. To maintain consistent momentum,
the gap between two consecutive meetings never
exceeds 120 days. The notice, detailed agenda
together with relevant notes and other material
information are generally shared with the directors
sufficiently in advance of Board meetings, enabling
effective deliberations and informed decision-
making.
During the financial year under review, the Board met
8 (eight) times and all the directors of the Company
attended at least one board meeting during such
period. Further, the details of the Board meetings,
AGM held during the financial year and directors’
attendance thereat, are provided below:

Company Overview


Independent Directors
The independent directors are vital to the efficacy
of the Board. They bring objective, unconflicted,
and diverse external perspectives to the Board and
thereby elevate the quality of strategic decision-
making. Their presence ensures that the Board
delivers balanced, unbiased judgments that robustly
safeguard the interests of all stakeholders, while their
independent oversight serves as the cornerstone of
good governance and sustainable corporate growth.
Since the Board is chaired by independent director,
the SEBI Listing Regulations require at least one-
third (33.33%) of the Board to comprise independent
directors. Accordingly, the Company comfortably
exceeds this requirement, as 4 (four) out of the 6 (six)
Board members (66.67%) are independent directors.
In accordance with Section 149 of the Act read with
Schedule IV thereto, Regulation 16 of the SEBI Listing
Regulations and other applicable laws, all independent
directors have provided declarations confirming that
they meet the prescribed criteria of independence,
and have, inter alia, confirmed that:
	 they are not material supplier, service provider or
customer or a lessor or lessee of the Company;
	 they have not been an employee, proprietor
or partner, of the firm of auditors or company
secretaries in practice, of the Company or its
subsidiaries in any of the three immediately
preceding financial years and the financial year
ended March 31, 2026;
	 they have not been an employee or proprietor or
a partner of any legal or consulting firm that had
business transactions with the Company or its
subsidiaries, amounting to 10 (ten) per cent or
more of the gross turnover of such firm, in any of
the three immediately preceding financial years
and the financial year ended March 31, 2026;
	 apart from receiving director’s remuneration
(including sitting fees), there have not been any
material pecuniary relationship or transactions
with the Company or its subsidiaries or their
promoters or directors during the three
immediately preceding financial years and
financial year ended March 31, 2026, exceeding
the limits specified under the Act and SEBI Listing
Regulations;
	 they have not been linked to a non-profit
organisation that receives significant contribution
from the Company or its directors or its subsidiaries
or that holds 2 (two) per cent or more of the total
voting power of the Company;
	 they are not aware of any circumstance or situation,
which exists or may be reasonably anticipated, that
could impair or impact their ability to discharge
duties with an objective independent judgement
and without any external influence;
	 neither they nor their relative(s) have held the
position of a key managerial personnel in the
Company or its subsidiaries in any of the three
immediately preceding financial years and the
financial year ended March 31, 2026.
Accordingly, based on the declarations received
from all independent directors, the Board has
confirmed that, in their opinion, independent
directors of the Company are the persons
of integrity, possess relevant expertise and
experience and fulfil the conditions specified
in the Act and SEBI Listing Regulations and are
independent of the management.
Meeting of Independent Directors
A separate meeting of the independent directors
was held on March 12, 2026, without the presence
of non-independent directors or representatives of
management. All the independent directors attended
the meeting.
At the meeting, the independent directors, inter alia,
reviewed and evaluated the aspects related to:
	 The performance of the non-independent
directors and Board as a whole;

3.	Committees of the Board
The Board has constituted several committees, each
entrusted with specific scope of responsibilities as
provided in the terms of reference of the respective
committee. Through detailed deliberations and
decisive actions within their respective domains,
the committees facilitate timely outcomes and
recommendations to the Board, thereby supporting
the Board in effective discharge of its duties.
As on March 31, 2026, the Board has 7 (seven)
committees namely, Audit Committee, Nomination
and Remuneration Committee, Stakeholders’
Relationship Committee, Risk Management
Committee, Corporate Social Responsibility
Committee, Investment Committee and Fund Raising
Committee, comprising of the Board members.

were present at the 15th AGM of the Company held on

utilization of the issue proceeds as it may, in its absolute discretion deem fit, without being required to
seek any further consent or approval of the Board or the members or otherwise.

6. Means of communication
The Company maintains transparent communication with its shareholders through dedicated channels, including annual
general meetings, periodic letters, newspaper publications, and investor conferences. These initiatives are designed to
enhance dialogue, strengthen relationships, and promote long-term sustainable value for all stakeholders.
	 The quarterly financial results, shareholders’ letters, annual and event-based stock exchange filings are publicly
accessible on the websites of NSE at www.nseindia.com, BSE at www.bseindia.com and website of the Company at
www.eternal.com.
	 The information regarding the availability of financial results, together with QR code and web link for accessing such
financial results, is generally published in Financial Express (English newspaper) circulating substantially in the whole
of India and in Jansatta (vernacular (Hindi) newspaper) in Delhi.
	 The Company provides quarterly updates to the shareholders/ investors on the business performance and financial
results through the shareholders’ letters. The Company also hosts earnings conference calls every quarter to address
investor queries on the Company’s performance and financial results. During the year under review, the Company
conducted 4 earnings conference calls. Audio recordings and transcripts of these calls are available at Link.
	 The Company’s AGM for the financial year ended on March 31, 2025 was conducted through video conferencing. The
transcript of the proceedings is available at Link.
	 The Company’s website contains a dedicated section “Investor Relations”. This section is a comprehensive database
of information including financial results, shareholders letters, annual reports of the Company, shareholder awareness
materials, etc. in an easily accessible manner. Further, the information in terms of Regulation 46 of the SEBI Listing
Regulations is also provided under this section available at Link.
	 The Company has designated e-mail ID: companysecretary@eternal.com for redressal of investors grievances.

Prevention of insider-trading practices
The Company has adopted the Code of Practices
and Procedures for Disclosure of Unpublished Price
Sensitive Information formulated in accordance with
SEBI (Prohibition of Insider Trading) Regulations,
2015, and fairly adhered to the principles described
therein.
The Company promotes and conducts several
awareness programmes throughout the year  to
reinforce the importance of preventing insider trading
through utilizing various channels such as web-based
modules, in-person training sessions, screensavers,
and targeted internal communications. These
programs educate employees on the complexities
of dealing in shares of the Company and strive to
prevent them from any violations. The ultimate goal
is to foster a robust culture of compliance, integrity,
and accountability.
Key areas covered in these programs included:
	 Educating employees on the confidentiality of
Unpublished Price Sensitive Information (“UPSI”)
and its ethical handling.
	 Implementation of mandatory assessment on core
policy elements that employees must pass before
seeking approval to deal in shares of the Company.
	 Ensuring clear, accurate, and timely communication
regarding the opening and closing dates of trading
windows.
	 Extensive training on buying and selling of Eternal
shares, including comprehensive guidance on
contra trading restrictions:
179
Financial Statements
Statutory Reports: CGR
Company Overview


Definition & Restrictions: Clear instruction that
employees are strictly prohibited from entering
into an opposite transaction (e.g., selling shares
recently purchased, or buying shares recently
sold) within a mandatory holding/lock-in period
(6 months) from the date of the prior trade.
Approvals & Consequences: Guidance on the
strict process for seeking approvals from the
Compliance Officer, and the severe penalties
and  disgorgement of profits that apply if an
unauthorized contra trade is executed.
	 Mandatory requirement to obtain formal pre-
clearance approval from the Compliance Officer
prior to dealing in shares of the Company.
	 Guidance on securely reporting actual or suspected
code violations, ensuring strict whistleblower
confidentiality throughout the process.
